(119) Flooding first, pulling fire teams (118) also serves the Counter need (it may rank first at a critical heel, sorted before the pairs): one team, not a pair; the section with the counter team is never a source.

(120) Engine out -> priority repair. `ContactIntel.EngineSystem(comp)` (the Engine module in use, inoperable or destroyed, else null); `DcAuto.Allocate` (before the section loop) finds the section whose `_listOfSystems` holds it and calls `SysPriority.Mark(sc, system)` (the property setter, so the Harmony patches of (105) keep the list; refuses lost / not repairable / undamaged systems; with SeveralPriorities off the engine replaces a "!" chosen by hand in that section; with it on the engine joins the list). Once per outage (`Ship.EngineMarked`, reset when the engine works again), so taking the mark off by hand sticks. Works only while Auto DC runs (mod on, transfers on); no option. Not tested in the game.

(121) Pairs rule (118/119) fixed after a trace without a single move: it needed two teams that fight a fire alone, so with one such team nothing moved. Now it moves as many as the section lacks (gap), two at most, one is enough to complete a pair. Diagnostic trace line every 10 s while the rule is looked at ("Auto DC (Flooding): section i lacks N team(s), teams that fight a fire alone: C, reserve P"). The trace of that run (ship 2, Flooding set at 15:26:26, trace ends 4 s later = still within the 4 s debounce) had no "Auto DC:" line at all.

(122) Pairs rule, second fix (save 4a0b4333-4.sav, ship Kresta: 4 teams, one each in sections 2/1/3/0, holes in 1-3). In Flooding first a team that is alone in a burning section is no longer "held" by that section's holes/water (`holds[j]=false`, unless it is the counter team), so the free teams (4) are shared by DcShare among the leaking sections (Want 2 each, rank = leak rate): 2+2 for the two leakiest, the rest give theirs up. Donors in the fallback: burning, one team, nothing planned for it (`target[j] < here[j]`), stayed >= 5 s - wet sections included, as long as their own plan is 0. Once a section has two teams it is no donor (no ping-pong). The first version took donors only from burning sections WITHOUT holes - none of the 4 in that save (3 had holes, section 0 was the only candidate and 2 were needed). Not tested in the game.

(123) Plugging holes <-> pumping: same hold as (117), 30 s (`LeakPumpHold`, `Ship.LpTask/LpSince`), checked first; the hull <-> pumping hold (60 s) works beside it.

(124) Trace line every 10 s while holes leak: "Auto DC counterflooding: list L / red R, trim T / red R, ratio X (on at 0.4), holes leak, on .., critical ..". Added after a trace (Flooding first, pairs worked) where nobody counterflooded: the trace has no heel values; Auto DC counterfloods only from 40% of the red zone (Kresta: deck points touch the sea at list 30.9 -> from about 12 deg), and only while a hole still lets water in.

(125) Critical jobs take teams off fire / flooding work that goes on (DcAuto.Allocate, before DcShare): only counterflooding at a critical heel (`Need.Crit` = `CounterCrit`) and the repair of an engine that is out of order (PrioSys need of `Ship.EngineSec`, set when the engine is marked). Held teams were subtracted from the free count, so with every team busy these got nothing. Now, if the free teams do not cover them (one team each, after the cover of the teams held in their section), sections whose best work ranks below the critical rank (not the critical sections, no counter team there) give up one team each (`donor[]`, `target--`, `left++`): worst rank first, then more teams. The gap-fill loop lets donors lose a team (`holds[j] && !donor[j]`, keep-one rule off for them). A counterflooding that is not critical (and other priority systems) waits for free teams. Once the counter team works (`CounterAct != 0`) its section holds it. Not tested in the game.

(126) Checked the pairing at a fire (rule 3: other work in a burning section gets >= 2 teams, the first fights the fire): the Wants and `CheckPrefix` are unchanged by (118)-(125); the Flooding regrouping takes only teams that are alone in their section; the critical donors (125) now spare a burning section with exactly two teams (fire fighter + worker) in a first pass and break it up only if nobody else can give a team.

(127) Trace: "Counterflooding in section i: action N (...), list/trim" whenever the counter team's action in a section changes (DcControl.CounterFlood, Ship.LastAct). Trace of the Kresta save (Flooding first): counterflooding started at ratio 0.40 (trim 1.2 of 3.0), team to section 0 (50 s); the heel kept growing to 0.60 afterwards, the action of the team is unknown until this line is in a trace.

(128) Counterflooding never started in the Kresta trace (Flooding first): the one team sent to the counter section 0 found a fire there and, by the fire-first rule of `CheckPrefix` (rule 2), fought it; the counter step needed a second team (Want 2 = fire bonus + counter) that DcShare never gave. Now `CheckPrefix` orders the two steps by the priority: counterflooding first where it ranks above fire - Flooding first, or a critical heel (`Ship.CounterCrit`, `SetPlan(..., critical)`) - fire first otherwise (Auto: Fire Rate > Fires > Counterflooding, Fire first). So in Auto / Fire first with a non-critical heel a lone team in a burning counter section still fights the fire. Not tested in the game.

(129) The "Auto Damage Control Status" line and the hints of the DC Priority buttons show what is first right now: `DcAuto.NowFirst(comp)` ("Counterflooding (critical heel)", "Engine repair", "Priority systems" joined by " > "; from `Ship.CounterCrit` / `EngineNow` / `PrioNow`, set in Allocate) goes in front as "Now first: {0}." + the mode's order text. In Auto the line shows the mod's order (new text "DC Priority: Auto - ...", row 571) instead of the game's own text while `DcTeams.TransfersOn` (the mod's Auto DC works); otherwise the game's text stays. Button hints are refreshed when the "now" text changes (`ViewState.TipNow`). The chain names stay English in every language (as the other chains). New rows 570-571 (en.tsv + 8 tr files, 571 texts). Not tested in the game.

(130) Status line reworked (RenderHelp): no description text any more, only the order of the ship's DC Priority as coloured runs (`OrderNames` / `OrderBits` per mode; names stay English): critical jobs first in red (`DcAuto.Critical`: 1 counterflooding at a critical heel - then it is left out of the chain, 2 engine repair, 4 other priority systems), the work that damage control can do now in yellow (`DcAuto.Avail`: Av* bits set in Allocate per ship: leak, fire growth, fire, water, hull that can be repaired (integrity to restore > 0), systems repairable, counterflooding on), the rest in the text colour. Shown while `DcTeams.TransfersOn`; otherwise the game's own text. The old OrderText rows (4 strings + the Auto row added in (129)) are unused now (`old` in en.tsv). Button hints keep the "Now first:" text. Not tested in the game.

(131) Fire under water (SubmergedFlooding.PutOutFires): a stricter condition - both deck points of the section (port and starboard) have to be under the sea (`dPort > 0 && dStar > 0`; before: the mean of the two > 0, so one side under water was enough). The rate is still SubmergedFireDecay % x the mean depth of the two points. cfg description + 8 translations changed in place (row 8). Not tested in the game.

(132) Counterflooding is critical whenever it is on (`ship.CounterCrit = ship.CounterOn`; was: ratio >= 0.8 of the red zone): water still comes in through holes and the list / trim is at 40% of its red zone (off at 20%) - that is critical as such (user: "flooding through the side is already a sign of criticality"). So it ranks first (-4), takes one team off fires / floods that go on (critical donors, (125)), goes before the fire in its section (`CheckPrefix`, (128)) and shows red in the status line. Trace of the Kresta save: counterflooding was on from 16:37:01 (list 12.8 of 30.9), all 4 teams were held by leaks in sections 1 and 2, nobody came for 5 minutes. `DcControl.HeelCritical` (0.8 / 0.6) is unused now. Not tested in the game.

(133) Counterflooding on from the red zone: `HeelOn` 1.0 (was 0.4), `HeelOff` 0.5 (was 0.2) of the start of the red zone of LIST / TRIM (still only while a hole lets water in). Replaces the 40% / 20% of (107) and (132). Not tested in the game.

(134) Counterflooding heel limits are live. `DcControl.Red` takes the red-zone starts from `SubmergedFlooding.Margins` (deck point touching the sea from the PRESENT pose: flooding already in the ship = deeper draft = smaller limit), capped by the dry limit (`DamageControlUi.ListRed/TrimRed`, computed once at load). Used by HeelRatio (on/off), Score (dead zone scale) and the trace line. Before, the trigger ignored the water in the ship while the dial already showed the live limit.

(135) Counterflooding is flooding only: `Score` no longer allows the pump actions (ActPumpPort / ActPumpStar are switched off, `can[...] = false`). Pumping out is the Pump task. A team ordered to counterflood with nothing to let in works by priority as before (pumping only via PumpSafe). Left as it was: the cfg description of AutoCounterflood still says "or pumping it out" (and 40%/20%) - stale, needs a localized text update (loc rows) when the options are reworked.

(136) Critical counterflooding re-ranks the flooding work (DcAuto.Allocate, `ship.CounterCrit`): Leak / Pump needs get `Gain` = how much the job takes off the list / trim (`DcControl.PumpGain` = best pump score of the section's wet sides, `LeakGain` = minus the flood score of the sides that leak; both from `Score`, parts of the red zone). Gain < `HeelGainMin` (0.02) -> Rank+1 (behind the helping jobs of its kind); within one rank the larger Gain goes first (before ByRate / size). If at least one job helps, a section that ONLY pumps (no hole, no fire, not the counter section, no counter action) and whose pumping makes the heel worse (Gain < -0.02) is released from `holds`: its teams are free and go to the helping sections. Trace line "Auto DC: counterflooding is critical, the teams pumping in section N make the heel worse". Not touched: the task choice inside a section (CheckPrefix).

(137) Trace only: the "Counterflooding in section N: action K" line now also shows the ship's level / danger band and the water in the port / starboard side of the section against the CounterFloodLimit (CounterWhy). Reason: a trace showed the action flipping 2 <-> 0 every fixed step after 90 s of flooding (list stuck at 10.3); the cause (level at the danger band or the side at its limit, or something else) is not visible without these numbers.

(138) Trace: "Auto DC teams: 0: here[+walking]->target flags | ... | pool N, free N, mode M[, counter critical]" - every Auto DC step where it changes and at least every 30 s (DcAuto.Allocate, before the gaps are filled). Flags: L hole leaks, W hole or water (so L comes with W), F fire, H held (teams stay), C the counterflooding section. Together with the existing "DC team moved" lines it shows who stood where and why nobody moved.

(139) (a) Critical counterflooding sheds the pumping teams at once: a section that ONLY pumps (no hole, no fire, not the counter section) is released from `holds` when its Gain is below half of the best Leak/Pump Gain (was: only when pumping there made the heel worse). Trace text changed accordingly. (b) `Score`: a side that reached CounterFloodLimit is not flooded again until the water is down by 1% (`mg`, only when the section is not at it already) - the trace showed action 2 <-> 0 every step after 90 s of flooding: the side most probably sat at its limit (a team plugging holes pumps a little at the same time). Hypothesis: check with the CounterWhy numbers (stbd water / limit) in the next trace. NOTE: the LEVEL gauge shows 100 * water / SinkAt; LevelNow and LevelDangerAt are absolute (% of the ship): gauge 41% is not a reason to stop flooding (danger band = gauge 80%).

(140) DcControl: the game's `_maxFlooding` of a side is ignored by counterflooding (always 100%): the only limit is CounterFloodLimit (Score, CounterFlood, CounterWhy). The field is still read (_maxFlood) for the availability check only.

(141) Trace (CounterWhy): also the LEVEL gauge as the UI shows it (100 * FloodingPercentage / SinkAt), the plain average of the sides' water, and SinkAt - to tell which value the level rule (LevelNow < 0.8 * SinkAt) really sees. The user says two different levels (absolute = the sides' average, relative = the gauge) are being compared; the code reads the game's FloodingPercentage in both the gauge and the rule.

(142) The level rule of counterflooding (Score.levelOk) now uses the gauge value itself: `DamageControlUi.LevelPct(comp)` (100 * FloodingPercentage / SinkAt, clamped to 100, the expression the LEVEL gauge draws) < `LevelDangerPct` (80, the start of the gauge's red band). Same arithmetic as before (fp < 0.8 * SinkAt), but one source for the gauge and the rule. If the trace still shows the rule blocking while the gauge reads well below 80, the "gauge N %" of CounterWhy and the gauge on screen disagree - look there.

(143) Critical heel: the bottleneck pumping job is critical too (DcAuto.Allocate, CounterCrit block): the Pump need with the best Gain (>= HeelGainMin, not the counter section) gets Crit = true and Rank -3, so the existing donor logic may take teams off other work for it - also the second team of a pair in a burning section with a hole (pass 1; the fire fighter stays). Trace once per change: "critical heel, pumping in section N is critical". Critical = counterflooding, the engine repair, and this one; the rest of the work cannot hold teams against them.

(144) Ranks of the critical jobs: counterflooding -4, engine repair -4 (was -2: now as important as counterflooding), the heel bottleneck pumping -3 (behind both), other priority systems -2 (not critical). Engine repair keeps its own donor rules (Crit). A tie of -4: the usual order of the share (a section with a team first, then by size).

(145) Critical jobs get all the teams they want, not one: (a) the donor deficit counts each critical need's Want (PrioSys: 1 + fire bonus) instead of 1 per need - a burning bottleneck section asks a pair (fire fighter + pumper), so both teams of a pair elsewhere may come (pass 1 takes the second team too); (b) before DcShare the critical needs (rank order) take their teams from `left` first, so the half-and-half rule of DcShare (rule 1) no longer sends one of them to the second-ranked job. Seen in game: the centre's pair split, one to the stern (fought the fire there: first team in a burning section) and one to the next leaking section.
(146) Trace for the 30% stop: while a counterflooding team lets water in, every 10 s "Counterflooding in section N (side, 10 s): the team let in X, something else changed it by Y, now Z (cap, game _maxFlooding, teams here, rate, dt)". Y < 0 = water taken out by something outside the counterflooding code between two steps; X ~ 0 = the team adds nothing (rate / teams).

(147) The counterflooding team pumped out the side it floods (seen by the user: it switches between flooding and pumping all the time): when CounterFlood has nothing to do in a step (act 0) the team is not used up and falls to the free group, whose Orders pump the side with more water - the very side being flooded. Fix: while counterflooding is critical (CounterCritNow), every free group of the ship pumps only via PumpSafe (not the high side, not the end already up) and plugs holes with the safe pump share (_safeLeak) - the 30 s / 60 s hold branches too. Also Score.levelOk has a hysteresis: flooding stops at the gauge's 80% and starts again below 78% (when the section is not at it already), so the action does not flip at the edge.

(148) Trace: "Counterflooding in section N: nothing to do (reason): flood port S ok/no, flood stbd S ok/no; LevelPct (stop 80), FloodingPercentage, SinkAt; heel; CounterWhy" every 10 s per section while Plan picks no action. Reason: the trace of build 1525 has no counterflooding action line at all - section 0 (the counter section, 2 teams) never got an action since the load (LastAct starts at 0, so no change line), the teams fought the fire instead. The team moves of (145) worked (centre pair -> stern).

(149) Trace of build 1534: counterflooding in section 0 is blocked by the level rule - LevelPct 79.2-79.9 (FloodingPercentage 39.6-39.9, SinkAt 50), while the sides' average is 20.8 (-> 42%, about what the user sees on the LEVEL gauge: 41-43%). The game's sink check compares FloodingPercentage with SinkAt (Sink thresholds patch), so 79% is the real distance to sinking. Open question: why the gauge shows ~43% when it draws 100 * FloodingPercentage / SinkAt. Added a UI trace "LEVEL gauge: FloodingPercentage X / sinks at Y -> shows Z" every 10 s.

(150) Counterflooding's level rule uses `DcControl.LevelGauge` = 100 * (mean water of all compartment sides) / SinkAt - what the game shows as the ship's flooding (21%) and what the LEVEL gauge shows (42%, confirmed by the user). FloodingPercentage read during the game's Damage Control step (CheckPrefix) gave 39.6 at the same moment - about twice; the UI reads it outside that step and gets the right value. Not checked: other reads of FloodingPercentage in physics code (TrimFlooding.cs:215 - which context?) may have the same problem.

(151) Trace of build 1541: the stern team (section 4, water only, critical heel) repaired systems / hull instead of pumping: since (147) a critical heel pumps only via PumpSafe, and PumpSafe refused every after section whenever trim > +0.2 (stern up) - here trim +0.5 (red 2.8) against a list of 3-4 (red 1.0). PumpSafe now judges by Score's pump scores (list and trim weighed by their red zones, as counterflooding does): a side may be pumped when its score > 0; the old fixed rules only when Score says the ship is level enough. Also: a section released by the critical-heel rule (only pumping, little gain) may give up its last team (`released[]` in the gap filling: before, a section with work kept one team, so section 0's freed team never went to section 4 that lacked one); the release trace is written once per section, not every step.

(152) Score (counterflooding, PumpGain / LeakGain, PumpSafe) for ships with exact deck points judges by the deck edges, not by the angles: `EdgeError` (once per physics step per ship) takes SubmergedFlooding.Margins from the present pose, the tilt left to each edge (rp, rs, rt, rb), and gives a list-like error 0.5*(rs - rp) (+ = the port edge nearer the sea) and a trim-like error 0.5*(rt - rb) (+ = the bow edge nearer), scaled by the tilt left when the edges are even. So a low quarterdeck at the water counts as 'stern down' even with the bow a little down by the angle (user: 'nose down does not matter when the flooding points are computed'). Dead zones as before (degrees). Ships without exact points: the angles as before. Not changed: HeelRatio (on / off of counterflooding, angle / live red zone). HeelInfo in the trace shows the edge errors.

(153) The red zone of LIST / TRIM for counterflooding takes the side / end whose deck edge is NEARER the sea (the least tilt left from the present pose, Margins), not the one the angle points to (`Red(..., out remL, out remT)`). HeelRatio per axis = max(|angle| / live red start, 1 - tilt left to the nearest edge / dry limit): the second term sees an edge at the water when the angle is small or points the other way (trace: trim +0.4 took the bow's 2.8, ratio 0.14, while the quarterdeck was at the water). Note: a ship that sits deep but level also gets a high ratio from the second term - Score then finds nothing to do (no section), so no team is sent.

(154) The schematic's trend arrows are the Flooding rows' own (Col.AP / Col.AS, hysteresis included) instead of being recomputed from the rate without hysteresis - they differed when a rate hovered around a threshold.

(155) Bug from (153): HeelRatio divided |angle| by the red start of the NEARER edge even when the angle pointed the other way - trace (build 1616): list 0, trim +1.4 (bow down) but the stern edge nearer the sea -> trim red 0.5 -> ratio 2.8, counterflooding 'critical' for minutes with no section to work in (best section -1). Now the angle term counts only the angle toward the chosen edge (Red(..., out toL, out toT)); the edge term (1 - tilt left / dry limit) is unchanged. Same trace: FloodingPercentage read in physics was 150 (gauge 301%) - more proof it must not be used there.

(156) Priority repair (engine) never done once the teams had arrived: sysQuota (the teams CheckPrefix puts on Systems first) was filled only from teams newly given out (crit prefill / DcShare). On the next step the teams were in the section, held, and covered the PrioSys need - no new teams, quota 0 - so they worked by order (holes / fire) and the engine stayed broken (trace build 1616: 2 teams sent to section 1 for the engine, then fire / holes). Now the held teams that cover a PrioSys need count into sysQuota too (minus the fire fighter of its fire bonus).

(157) Fire first over the holes it keeps open (user's choice): `SectionEffects.LeaksHeldByFire(comp, i)` = the section burns and every leaking hole there is not above the fire's reopen level (FireReopenLimit % of its largest rate, +2%). Then Auto DC (DcAuto: the Leak job and AvLeak) and the teams' order (DcControl: free group incl. the 30 s leak/pump hold, and the counterflooding teams with nothing to let in) skip plugging - the fire is fought instead; once it is out, the holes are plugged as usual. A hole above that level (a fresh, larger one) is still plugged (down to it). Seen in a trace: 3 teams switched between the fire and a hole the fire reopened every 1-2 s. Hand-given tasks are not touched. anyLeak (counterflooding on while holes leak) unchanged.

* (158) Team speed: two new settings, `[FireAndFlooding] SystemRepairSpeed` = 2.0 and `HullRepairSpeed` = 1.0 (x game, settings window "Team speed": System repair, Hull repair). Harmony transpilers multiply `Crew._systemRepair` in `Compartments.RepairSystems` (2 places) and `Crew._structureRepair` in `RepairStructure` (1 place) - next to the DCTeamEffectiveness patch, like Extinguishing / Pumping out. Loc: 4 new texts (en.tsv + 8 tr/*.txt).

* (159) Fire at the engine: a fire damages every system of its section (`SystemCompartment.UpdateFireDamage`), so repairing an engine that is out of order while its section burns is wasted. DcAuto: while the engine section burns, its PrioSys job is replaced by a critical Fire job (Rank -4, Crit, Want max(3, 1+extra); the section's own Fire/FireRate needs are dropped) - donors and "critical jobs take teams first" bring the teams as for the repair; no sysQuota there. `SetPlan(..., fireAll)` -> DcControl `Ship.FireAll`: in that section every free team fights the fire (after the counterflooding team, before the systems quota). Once the fire is out the engine repair (PrioSys -4) goes on. Status / help line: "Fire at the engine" (Critical bit 8) instead of "Engine repair". Trace: "Auto DC: the engine's section N burns - the fire first ..." / "... the fire at the engine is out - its repair goes on". Fires in other sections are not affected (they do not damage the engine; spread into the engine section is not anticipated).

* (160) Fire at the engine, several sections: the game puts a system into every section its model spans (`_compartmentIndexMin..Max`, the same object in each `_listOfSystems`), so a fire in any of them damages the engine. DcAuto: each step, while the engine is out and its repair is still a priority (the mark on EngineSec, section not flooded), every section whose system list holds the engine and that burns is a bit of `fireAll`; each gets the critical Fire job (Rank -4, Want max(3, 1+extra)) and all its teams fight the fire (DcControl `FireAll` is now a bit mask). While any of them burns the engine repair (PrioSys in EngineSec) is not asked - even when EngineSec itself does not burn; status "Fire at the engine". Trace on every change of the set: "Auto DC: fire at the engine in section(s) a, b - the fire first ..." / "... the fires at the engine are out - its repair goes on".

* (161) Sinking triggers: the deck rule is now OR, not AND. Before: for ships with exact deck points the water level threshold (FloodLimit, 40%) counted only while `SinkAllowed` (both central compartments or 5 of 2N under the sea for DeckFloodingDelay 3 s), else it was out of reach. Now `SinkLevel` = -1 (sinks at once) when `SubmergedFlooding.SinkByDeck` (the same points, each under the sea for `SinkDelay` = 10 s in a row; false for ships without exact points / destroyed), else FloodLimit for every ship. DeckFloodingDelay (3 s) still starts the inflow over the deck. Trace once per ship: "Sinking by the deck: ...". `SinkAllowed` kept, unused. README (2. No instant sinking...) still says "sinks only when both central compartments (or 5 of the 10) are under the sea" - to be reworded.

* (162) Sinking by the deck: `SinkPoints` 5 -> 6 (both central deck points under the sea for 10 s, or at least 6 of the 2N).

* (163) Trace 20261104.1646 (Kresta, 4 teams, critical counterflooding in section 0): the heel bottleneck took both teams of section 2 to pump section 4 (gain 10.1); once its port water was out only the side that worsens the list was left, PumpSafe refused, and the pair fell through the order to Systems while sections 1-3 burned and leaked with no team. The release of pump-only sections needed bestGain > 0 and Gain < 0.5 bestGain - with no other helping flooding job it never fired. Now a pump-only, not burning, held section in critical counterflooding is set free also when its pump Gain < HeelGainMin (pumping there does not help the heel at all), whatever bestGain is; its teams go to the other work by the usual gap filling.

* (164) Flooding first: no fixed pair. The Leak / Pump need asked >= 2 teams in Flooding first even without a fire (118); now only a burning section asks 2 (fire fighter + worker, every mode). Without a fire a section asks 1 + extra (bad leak / fast rise) and the teams come in the order of the queue. The regrouping of teams that fight a fire alone (118-122) stays, but moves as many as the section lacks (no cap of 2); trace "Auto DC: a team for flooding in section N from the fire in section M".

* (165) Counterflooding (and its critical mode) goes off when there is nothing to level. Trace 1646 (19:57-19:58): ratio 0.65-0.68 (off only below HeelOff 0.5) while the deck edges were even (list-like -0.2 / 11, trim-like 0.0 / 1.8), best section -1 - the ratio was held up by the trim angle against a red zone moved by the ship sitting deep (LEVEL 49-50%), which counterflooding cannot help; the critical mode kept PumpSafe, the gain ranking and the bottleneck on. Now (DcAuto): on = HeelDanger (ratio >= HeelOn 1.0, holes leak) AND `CounterSection` >= 0; off = ratio < HeelOff, no leak, or `CounterSection` -1 for `CounterIdleOff` = 10 s in a row (trace "Auto DC: counterflooding off - nothing to level for 10 s (...)"); after that it needs the ratio >= 1.0 again with something to level. `CounterSection` fix: a section counted even when Best found no action (bs stayed at ScoreMin 0.02 > 0) - caps reached / LEVEL stop gave a section with nothing to do; now such sections are skipped (unless its team is at an action that is still allowed).

* (166) Trace 1701 (Kresta, critical counterflooding in section 0): after the fires of sections 0 and 4 went out, sections 1-3 burned and leaked with no team, section 0 kept 2 teams (the counterflooding one + DC4: water held it, pumping refused by PumpSafe - port side at its minimum, presumably - so it fell to hull / systems), section 4 pumped (gain 9.5). The release of (163) skipped the counterflooding section. Now the counterflooding section is released too when its pumping does not help (Gain < HeelGainMin or < half the best): its Counter need keeps the one counterflooding team (critical prefill), the other teams go to the work elsewhere. Trace: the "Auto DC teams" line now ends with "| doing 0:CH 1: ..." (per section, what each team did last tick: I idle, L plug, F fire, P pump, H hull, S systems, C counterflood) and at a critical heel "| pump gain s=g ..." (the line is still written only on a change of the plan or every 30 s).

* (167) New `[FireAndFlooding] FireGrowthLimit` (default 0.5, % of the fire's maximum a second; 0 = off; settings window Fire and flooding > Fire: "Fire growth limit"). The game caps the growth rate at _maxfireGrowRate = maxIntegrity/1000 against _maxFireSeverity = maxIntegrity/299, i.e. about 30% of the maximum a second. `FlameUpMul` (the FlameUpSpeed transpiler of SystemCompartment.OnFixedUpdate, called with the section) now also clamps the `_fireGrowRate` field to the limit (so spread fires, tooltips, Auto DC see the held rate) and caps the returned growth (after FlameUpSpeed and the speed effect). Only while the growth branch runs (_fireGrowRate > 0). Profiles: Base Game 0, Advanced 0.5; also added the missing SystemRepairSpeed / HullRepairSpeed to both profiles (Base Game 1.0 / 1.0, Advanced 2.0 / 1.0) - the trace warned about them. Loc: 3 new texts (578).

* (168) Trace 1715 (20:43-20:44): (a) the critical mode stayed on at ratio 0.60 with a best score of 0.08-0.14 - the trim-like edge error 0.3 deg sat just above the active team's narrower dead zone (0.15), so the team itself kept "something to level" alive; (b) the counterflooding section flipped 0 <-> 1 every 2 s (equal scores), each flip released the other section's team (166) and the counter need pulled a team back - a team was walked 0 -> 2 and later 2 -> 0 (taken off a fire as a critical donor), another 1 -> 2. Now: `CounterUseful` = 0.25 (parts of the red zone): counterflooding (and its critical mode) starts only when the best section scores at least that, and goes off after `CounterIdleOff` 10 s below it (trace "counterflooding off - it takes off less than 0.25 ..."). The counterflooding section is sticky (`Ship.CounterKeep`): the last one is kept while its score > 0 and the best is not 1.5x better. New `DcControl.CounterScore(comp, i)` (the per-section score; CounterSection uses it). The section / score chosen at the on/off check is the one planned (no second CounterSection call).

* (169) Trace 1747 (21:03-21:04, Auto): 3 teams pumped (section 0: 2, section 1: 1) while section 3 (holes + fire at the engine, critical, 1 team) and section 4 (fire, no team) burned. Two causes. (a) Water held its teams against everything not critical, also against fires that the order puts before water. Now a held pump-only section (water, no hole, no fire; not the counterflooding section) gives way when a need of a better rank (not pumping) in another section has fewer teams here + coming than it wants: `holds[j] = false`, the gap filling keeps one team there (the work rule), the others may go. Trace once per section: "Auto DC: pumping in section N gives way to work of a higher rank that lacks teams (section M, fire) ...". (b) `DcControl.CounterAct` (CState.Act) kept the last counterflooding action after the mode went off (sections 0 and 1 had counterflooded) - those sections counted as "at counterflooding": never critical donors (so the engine fire got no teams) and held. `SetPlan` now clears Act of every section but the planned counterflooding one. `DcAuto.KindName` for the trace.

* (170) Trace 1806: the pump-only sections 0 and 1 gave way (169) to the fire at the engine (section 3, wants 3+, target 4), but only one team came: the gap filling keeps one team in a section with work, and the yielded sections were not critical donors either (not held). Now a yielded section is also `released[j]`: none of its teams is kept for the water while the higher work lacks teams. Once that work has its teams (here + coming >= Want) the water holds its section again.

* (171) Engine out = engine only. While the engine is out of order and its repair is a priority (the mark on EngineSec, section not flooded), every team works at NoEngineTeamEffectiveness on everything but the systems, so DcAuto now drops every job except the fires at the engine (`fireAll`) and the engine repair (PrioSys in EngineSec; it waits while a fire at the engine burns, 160). Those jobs get `Need.All` (Crit, Rank -4) and share every team that does not counterflood (Want = an even share of `usable` minus the counter team; the crit prefill and the donor count use Want also for PrioSys); every other section is unheld and released (keeps no team). Counterflooding at a critical heel keeps its one team (an exception, the user said "nothing else" - to be confirmed). The repair quota = the teams given to the PrioSys job, so in the engine section they all repair systems. Trace on change: "Auto DC: the engine is out - every team goes to ..." / "the engine works again ... the teams go back to the other work".

* (172) Encyclopedia hung on the open life raft (trace 1812: raft spawned, 1 compartment, "deck points touch the flat sea at list 8.6", Heel log list -13.5 .. +9.6 rocking; no error). Likely (161): the deck sinking rule for a 1-compartment ship is the "middle section" rule - its two rim points sit at the water, rocking keeps them under for 10 s, and SinkLevel returned -1, sinking the raft in the viewer. `SinkByDeck` now returns false for fewer than 2 compartments. Not confirmed that this was the hang (no exception in the logs).

* (173) Sinking by the deck: only ships with 5 sections (`SinkByDeck`: n != 5 -> false), not "2 or more".

* (174) Engine repair where the teams are. Trace 1851: the fire at the engine in section 3 went out (21:54:50) and all 4 teams walked 70 s to section 1 (the section marked first) to repair the engine - section 3 holds the engine too. The game repairs in a section only its own "!" system (else the first damaged one of its list), so the mark itself moves: new `SysPriority.IsMarked(sc, system)` and `SysPriority.Move(from, to, system)` (old section set to null - the game clears its flag - then Mark on the new one). DcAuto: while the engine is out, its mark is on EngineSec and no section of the engine burns, the repair section becomes the not flooded engine section with the most teams (strictly more than the current one; a flooded current one always gives way); trace "Auto DC: the engine repair moves from section A to section B". The engine logic now needs the engine itself to be the marked system of EngineSec (was: any pending mark there).

* (175) No rest for fatigue when the ship is in a bad way (`DcTeams.Heavy` = ContactIntel.Heavy: sinking, or water / hull / fire at stage 3+, e.g. FloodingPercentage >= 20). Trace 1856: at 22:00:49 and 22:01:37 all 4 teams went to rest exhausted (fatigue 2, health 0.6-0.97) with the ship at about 25% water. Now `RestPull` sends a team to rest only for a heavy wound (health < RestHealth) while heavy; in the reserve a team is marked resting for fatigue only when not heavy, and a resting team comes back when heavy as soon as its health is >= HeavyHealth (50%), whatever its fatigue (before: also needed fatigue level 0) - teams resting for fatigue alone are back at once. Not heavy: unchanged.

* (176) Trace 1903 (critical counterflooding, ratio 1.00 from the ship sitting deep): DC3 alone in section 2 (water only, the middle section) repaired systems: at a critical heel pumping goes through PumpSafe, which allowed a side only when its pump score was > 0 - the middle section does not trim and the list was in its dead zone, so both scores were 0 and pumping was refused. Now PumpSafe allows a score >= 0 (pumping that does not make the list / trim worse); less water lifts a ship that sits deep. Pumping that worsens the heel is still refused.

* (177) Free teams join work beyond its plan, counterflooding included. Trace 1903: DC3 stayed in section 2 (water, pumping gain 0 at a critical heel - released) while counterflooding had one team and the stern (holes + water) two. Rule 7 (one more team every ExtraEvery s, up to ExtraMax 4 per section) took teams only from the reserve or a section with no work at all, and gave up when its first pick (fewest teams) had no source. Now: candidates = one per section (its best ranked need), fewest teams first, then rank; the first one some team can come to gets it; sources also a section whose best work ranks below the pick, is not held and has a team its plan does not need (released: any). Counterflooding: `SetPlan(..., counterN)` -> `DcControl.Ship.CounterN` = teams in the counterflooding section minus what its other work wants (at least 1); CheckPrefix lets that many counterflood (the flood rate counts the teams: teams x rate x CounterRate). Trace "Auto DC: one more team for section N (job) from ...".

* (178) Trace 1915: back and forth 2 -> 0 (rule 7, "one more team for counterflooding") and 0 -> 2 a second later (gap filling: section 2's pumping wanted its team back), twice; also the bottleneck pumping of section 2 (critical, gain 0.28) was at the same time "released" (gain < half of the best 1.87). Now: rule 7 runs only when no section lacks teams of its plan (here + coming >= target everywhere) and takes a team from a section with work only if that section has more teams than its plan (`here > target`; the released shortcut is gone) - the plan's own teams stay; the release of pump-only sections skips a critical need (the bottleneck).

* (179) No debounce on the first pass: the damage found by the first Allocate of a ship (a loaded game; `Ship.Primed` false) counts at once (`Seen = now - Debounce`), so every job is planned together - trace 1915: only the critical counterflooding was planned at load, two teams of the reserve went to it, and the jobs of sections 2 and 4 came 4 s later.

* (180) (179) reverted: the debounce applies on the first pass again. Trace 1925: no gain - after a load the ship settles deeper by the stern within seconds (the saved state does not seem to hold the real trim: ratio 1.00 at load, 3.4-4.7 a few seconds later), so the jobs change right after the first pass anyway.

* (181) List / trim saved and restored (new src/AttitudeSave.cs). The game saves GeoPosition (with the depth) and Heading only (Compartments.LoadStateFromFile: water, fires, sink delay); a loaded ship stands on an even keel and settles again within seconds (save 7.sav, Kresta: trace 1925 ratio 1.0 -> 4.7, stern down, LEVEL 52 -> 66% as the stern edge went under). DcSavePostfix writes "AlternativeDamageModel_Attitude" = pitch,roll (hull localEulerAngles x, z, wrapped to +-180); DcLoadPostfix (mod on) stores it; the first Compartments.OnFixedUpdate of that ship (TeamTickPrefix) sets x / z back keeping the heading, sets the Rigidbody rotation (by reflection: UnityEngine.PhysicsModule not referenced) and clears its angular velocity. Skipped for a sinking ship or |pitch| > 60 / |roll| > 80. Trace "Attitude restored after the load: pitch P, roll R (it was ...)". Works only with saves made by this build. Not tested in the game.

* (182) Trace 1952 (8.sav): (a) the attitude restore works ("Attitude restored ... pitch -1.5, roll -0.8" for the Kresta), but 8.sav was made 7 s after loading 7.sav (its Trend starts at 0 s), while the ship was still settling - a save made after it has settled is needed to judge it. (b) DC3 in Midships repaired systems: its water was only on the side whose pumping makes the heel worse (gain -0.15), PumpSafe refused, yet the Pump job stayed in the plan and held the team. (c) DC2: the extra counterflooding team (rule 7, from the reserve) did Systems in section 0 (the section's own Pump job, gain -2.2, took it as "other work" - counterN 1), then the gap filling took it as a surplus to the stern (100 s). Now at a critical heel Pump jobs with gain < 0 (not the bottleneck) are dropped from the plan (their teams are free); and the Counter need wants every team of its section beyond what the other work there wants (`counts[cs] - other`, at least 1) - teams that joined counterflooding are part of the plan, not a surplus.

* (183) Trace 1958 (23:05-23:06): (a) the counterflooding team of section 0 was taken (23:05:45) for section 2's pumping by the fallback "the first team for fire / flooding may come off hull or systems work" - it looked only at holds, and the counterflooding section was not held (its water was released); counterflooding then had no team for 30 s and a team walked back from section 2 (23:06:14). The fallback now takes a team only from a section with more teams than its plan, or whose best work ranks below the job (`SecRank`). (b) section 2's pump gain hovered around 0 (+-0.00x): the Pump job was dropped and re-added every second, so the one free team's target flipped between section 2 (pumping) and section 3 (systems), two walks, and the team repaired systems in between. Now `PumpTolerance` = 0.05: the job is dropped only below -0.05, and PumpSafe allows a pump score >= -0.05 (a hair worse heel is no reason to stop pumping that lifts the ship).

* (184) Small pumping is no job. `SmallWater` = 10% of a compartment (FloodingDelta, water above its minimum): below it the Pump kind does not count in the section's order loop (the next kind of its work - systems, hull - counts instead), and the water does not hold the section's teams (holds uses `bigWater`). The teams there keep pumping by the order (CheckPrefix) while nobody needs them elsewhere; free teams may still be sent for it by rule 7, after every other candidate and only where nobody is (`smallPumps`, rank 1000). The critical heel's bottleneck is chosen among the real Pump jobs only (small water has little heel gain anyway). A constant, no setting.

* (185) Aft leaks grew with two teams plugging (hint: Holes inflow rising). Game bug: FloodingCompartment.OnFixedUpdate grows an unattended leak (integrity < 19%, no leak stop for 300 s) by 0.001/s while `DamageControlTeamsNumbers[_compartmentNumber] < 1` - _compartmentNumber is 1-based (Comp.cs: `= l + 1`), so section i looked at section i+1 and the aft section at index 5, the reserve (pool 0 in the trace -> growth on both sides; RepairFloodingRate plugs one side at a time). New `LeakTeamIndexTranspiler`: after the one `ldfld _compartmentNumber` followed by `ldelem.i4` (checked offline in Seapower-Scripts.dll, IL_018a) calls `LeakTeamIndex(n)` = n - 1 while the mod is on (its own section). Trace "Unattended leak team index: 1 places patched".

* (186) The hull holds the water. Trace 2026: after the aft holes were plugged (23:36:52) its three teams repaired systems (SSS) while the section kept its water: the game keeps MinimumFlooding = (100 - integrity%) / 2 in a compartment (pumping stops at it, FloodingDelta 0 - no W flag), and only welding the hull lowers it (up to MaxAllowedIntegrity = lowest + 20%). The order put Systems before Hull. New `DcAuto.HullHoldsWater(comp, i)`: a side keeps >= SmallWater (10%) as its minimum and its hull can still be repaired (IntegrityDelta > 0). Then DcAuto plans a Hull job at the rank of pumping (in the order loop, where Pump has nothing), and DcControl's free group repairs the hull before the systems.

* (187) Structure before Systems in every mode (as the game's own order: "Flooding Rate > Fire > Flooding > Structure > Systems"). Trace 2041: DC4 in section 1 repaired systems after its water was at the hull's minimum (below SmallWater, so (186) did not apply); a hand-given Hull task (23:46:44) is cleared by Auto DC. DcAuto orders, DcControl.Orders, the status chain (OrderNames / OrderBits) and the three PriorityTips now put Structure (Hull) before Systems. Priority systems ("!", the engine) stay first as before. Loc: 3 new rows (579-581, translations = the old rows with the chain swapped; the chains are English in every language), 581 texts.

* (188) Order: ... > Flooding > Flooded Structure > Systems > Structure (user's choice; replaces (187)). Flooded Structure = hull repair in a section where the hull keeps water in: `HullHoldsWater` (a side's MinimumFlooding >= `FloodedHullMin` 1% - was SmallWater 10% - and IntegrityDelta > 0). DcAuto: new order kind `FloodHull` (101) between Pump and Systems in every mode, planned as a Hull job at that rank; plain Hull last again. DcControl.Orders back to Systems > Hull, with the hull-holds-water check before Systems (186). Status chain: "Flooded Structure" with `DcAuto.AvFloodHull` (128) set where the hull holds water. Loc: 3 new tip rows (582-584), 584 texts.

* (189) Trace 2050: DC4 (section 1, plan 0) kept repairing systems while section 0 wanted a second team (1->2) and the stern had the bigger water / hull job; free 3 but nothing moved: the gap filling never takes the last team of a section with work, and rule 7 waits while a section lacks teams - a deadlock. Now the gap filling takes that last team when its section's best work ranks below the job being filled (`SecRank(j) > n.Rank`; e.g. systems / structure against water or fire elsewhere); a section with a fresh, still debounced fire keeps its team. Once the plans are filled, rule 7 sends the remaining free teams (the stern included).

* (190) Critical state for rest (ContactIntel.Heavy, used only by DcTeams: no rest for fatigue, shortened rest) reworked by the user: sinking; or FloodingPercentage >= 20 (stage 3) while a hole still leaks (any compartment _floodingRate > 0); or hull integrity under 50% (model stage 3+) while something burns or leaks; or fires >= 50 in total, or a fire nobody fights (a burning section without a team that did the Fire task last tick: new `DcTeams.FireFought`). Before: water / hull / fire at stage 3 each on its own. Also `RestPull`: the last team fighting a fire in its section is not sent to rest for fatigue (only for a heavy wound).

* (191) Forecast card: "Critical" in red (sev 2) instead of "Stable" when ContactIntel.Heavy says the state is critical (190) and no sinking is forecast within 90 min. A forecast "Sinks in ~N min" keeps its text. Loc: "Critical" (row 585, 8 languages).

* (192) Leaks that come back. Trace 2101: the aft team went to rest (00:05:44) right after plugging; 30 s later the aft leak was open again (critical, rest cut short): the game reopens the leak of a compartment under 19% integrity with no team in its section once 300 s have passed since that side's leak was stopped (each side its own timer). New `DcAuto.LeakRisk(comp, i)`: a side under 19% (CurrentIntegrityPercent) whose hull can still be repaired. It counts as Flooded Structure (`HullHoldsWater` returns it too): a Hull job at that rank in DcAuto, hull before systems for the teams there, the status bit; the section keeps its last team against lower work (gap filling keepOne) and `RestPull` does not take its last team for fatigue (a heavy wound still does). Once the hull is above 19% (always reachable: MaxAllowedIntegrity >= lowest + 20%) the section is free.

* (193) Aft left empty. Trace 2110: at 00:14:02 the aft hole was plugged (Heavy needs a leak -> false), three teams went to rest at once, and the last aft team (only small water left, plain Structure work) was taken for Forward by the gap filling: `keepOne` only knew `work[j]`, not (192)'s LeakRisk, so the section was empty and its leak reopened 30 s later. Fixes: (1) gap filling and the "first team may come off hull/systems work" loop never take the last team of a LeakRisk section (hull under 19%, repairable) unless the job is critical (counterflooding / engine); (2) DcTeams.RestPull: the critical state (Heavy) holds 60 s after it ends (HeavyHold), so plugging the last hole does not send half the teams to rest and call them back.

* (194) Relief of tired teams. DcAuto (before rule 7): when the reserve has a fresh, ready team (DcTeams.PoolFresh) and a section with work has a tired team (fatigue 1+) and no fresh one (DcTeams.NeedsRelief; none on its way), one fresh team is sent there (every ExtraEvery s, up to ExtraMax teams in the section). DcTeams.RestPull: a tired team (fatigue 1+) for which a fresh team has arrived in its section later (FreshAfter) goes to rest at once, ignoring the critical state, the LeakRisk and last-fire-fighter holds (the section stays manned).

* (195) Pumping that worsens the list / trim, outside the critical heel too. Until now only the counterflooding emergency (CounterCritNow) made the teams pump through PumpSafe (refuses a side whose pumping lowers the high end / side); otherwise the game's pump ran unchecked, so a ship held down by unrepairable hull damage (stern in the water) lost the water that kept it level and trimmed deeper. Now every Auto DC pumping team goes through DcControl.PumpGuard: the game's pump while the ship is within the dead zone of the heel, PumpSafe once the list / trim is off it (the same score, tolerance -0.05). DcAuto (no critical heel): a Pump need with PumpGain < -PumpTolerance is dropped, a pump-only section is released (teams go to the next work), traced once as "pumping in section N would worsen the list / trim". Manual orders are not limited.

* (196) Release 1.7.0 prepared: ChangeList.txt / ChangeList-1000.txt rewritten for everything since 1.6.0 (entries (114)-(195)); README.md / README.txt / README.BBCode.txt updated (rest and relief, critical state, new order with Flooded Structure, no pumping that worsens the heel, engine out, Critical forecast, deck sinking rule, team repair speeds and fire growth limit, list / trim saved). Release/AlternativeDamageModel.dll rebuilt. Not committed (waiting for the user's command).

* (197) Unfinished small repairs. Trace 2133 (ends 00:37:26): sections 0, 1, 4 stayed without a team while light system damage (a team not on site counts only medium+ damage) and hull under HullMin (5%) were left in them; the teams sat in sections 2 and 3, and the one in section 4 went idle and walked away. DcAuto: per section `minorWork` (1 = any repairable system damage, 2 = hull > 0.01%) for sections without other work; when no other need exists anywhere (needs empty, not Fire first) these become the lowest Systems / Hull jobs (Rank = order.Length*2+2, Want 1), so free teams finish them. Normal work keeps the thresholds (no walks for trifles while real work exists).

* (198) Trace 2139 + screenshot (Berkut): forward flooding 64% was left (pumping there worsens the trim: stern edge near the sea, "would worsen" -0.32), and the forward systems / hull stayed unrepaired with two teams resting. Cause: the kind loop of DcAuto.Allocate takes the first kind with work per section (`break`); Pump counted as work (work[i] = true) although (195) dropped its need afterwards, so Systems / Hull of that section never got a need (and (197)'s minor work needs !work[i]). Now the heel check is made where the section is scanned: `heelBlock` (PumpGain < -PumpTolerance on a section with big water) = no Pump kind, no hold (`bigWater` false), not a small pump either; the section's next kind of work counts. `ship.HeelStop` (mask) only for the trace line. The later else-block of (195) removed.

* (199) Game bug: Compartments.RepairSystems(index) without a priority system takes the first system of `_listOfSystems` that is Repairable, not lost (destroyed without _alwaysRepairable) and not past its limit (Biggest <= Current) and adds integrity to it - also to a system at full integrity (BaseSystem.OnFixedUpdate clamps it back) - and returns true. So with an intact system first in the list: the systems task "works" for ever (trace: "S", the team icon idle because DamageSummary.IsRepairing sees no rise), damaged systems further down are never repaired, CheckDamageControl / our CheckPrefix never see the team idle (no return to the reserve, no rest), and Auto DC counts the team as busy. Trace 2139 + screenshot: teams in sections 2-4 on "S" for minutes doing nothing. Fix: new src/SysRepairFix.cs, called from the existing RepairSystems prefix (after SysPriority) + new postfix: no priority system -> the first system that passes the game's checks AND has integrity below its maximum is picked; none -> result false, original skipped; it is not the game's own pick -> it goes into the game's field `_systemPrioritisedForRepair` (field only: no "!", no SysPriority list) for this call, the game's priority branch repairs it (speed and effectiveness multipliers stay), the postfix clears the field. The game's side effect for systems passed in the loop (repaired past their worst level: Inoperable off, switched on; a non-targeting radar is left as is) is done for the systems before the pick.

* (200) SysRepairFix traces once per section and case what the game would have done: "nothing to repair - the game would 'repair' the intact system X for ever" and "the game would 'repair' the intact system X instead of the damaged Y". To confirm (199) in the game (the user has not noticed it in the base game nor in older versions).

* (201) Release 1.7.0 prepared again: ChangeList.txt / ChangeList-1000.txt and the three READMEs updated for (197)-(200) (small repairs finished, heel-blocked pumping does not hold the section's other work, systems repair fix). Release dll rebuilt. Not committed (waiting for the user's command).

* (202) ChangeLists rewritten as the net change since 1.6.0 (what a player sees), without the fixes made on the way during 1.7.0 development; the unconfirmed systems repair fix (199) is not mentioned. READMEs describe the mod against the base game (no change history).

* (203) DC team hint: the efficiency is that of the work the team does now (DamageControlUi.HealthNote(comp, health, fatigue, section, task)): the engine penalty is not applied while it repairs systems (the line "Engine out ..." is dropped then), and a team that does not fight a fire in its section works at FireWorkEfficiency - new line "Amid a fire it does not fight: N%" (DcControl.FireIn made public). The Efficiency line also appears without fatigue when it is below 100%. Reserve / moving teams: as before (no task). New text (586) translated in 8 languages.

* (204) System repair hint (DamageControlUi.Systems.cs, SystemTip): while repairing, a line "Teams repairing systems here: N, efficiency E%" (the teams of the section whose task is Systems, mean of health / fatigue efficiency, no engine penalty - it does not apply to systems; the rate above is the measured one and already includes it). While not repairing, the reason is named: another system of the section is repaired first (the game repairs one at a time), or the team there is busy with <task> (instead of the bare "Waiting for a team"); no team / flooded as before. Helpers SysSection, SysTeams. 3 new texts (587-589) in 8 languages.

* (205) Map ship damage icons (ContactIntel.Of, direct sight): the hull icon was already hidden when the hull is welded as far as it can be (HullWeldedUp); the flooding icon is now hidden too when no water above the compartments' minimum is left (all FloodingDelta <= 0.5, `NoPumpableWater`, no hole leaks), the ship is not sinking and nothing can be welded (hull icon off or HullWeldedUp): the water the hull keeps in is not a problem damage control can still solve. cfg description and READMEs updated. Applies to the Damage State tab of a contact (same Info).

* (206) (205) corrected: the "cannot be repaired any more" hiding (hull welded up, water the hull keeps in) is only for the own ship. A contact's Damage State / map icons show what the sensors tell by class; its repair plans are not known (this also removes the old HullWeldedUp use for contacts from 1.6.0). cfg description and READMEs worded accordingly.

* (207) Save of DC teams checked against the user's save (ca20d047-1.sav): the file holds the teams of every ship (`AlternativeDamageModel_Teams`: state / section / time left / walk time / rest / from / stay / health / fatigue / stun); a team in a section is written as `2/<section>/...` (Vessel3: two teams in section 3 with their stay), all teams of the own ship (Vessel1) were in the reserve at the save (`0/-1/..`, from sections 1 and 3). Hand-given tasks are in `AlternativeDamageModel_DC` only when Auto DC is off and a task is set (none in this save, Auto tasks are recomputed each tick). No fault found in Save/Load by reading; added the trace line "DC teams saved: ..." so a save + load trace shows whether the loaded state equals the saved one.

* (208) System repair hint (204) refreshed only on mouse moves; now the open tooltip is rebuilt with the other open tooltips (UpdateOpenTips -> UpdateOpenSysTip, 4 times a second): row.ToolTipOpening/Closing set `OpenSysTip`.

* (209) Teams walked from section to section every ~10 s (trace e4fb849e: Krivak, 3 teams, "one more team for section N (systems) from section M" and "a team for section M from section N" in turn; each walk 40-60 s). Two attempts (a 60 s stay guard; rule 7 taking only free teams) were both taken back: the guard is a patch for the effect, the second may break other logic. Rule 7 / gap filling are as before (193); the right change is still to be found - the cause is that the targets of the sections flip between ticks (section 2: target 0 with a team doing systems, next tick 1).

* (210) (208) did not work: UpdateOpenSysTip was called from UpdateOpenTips, which runs only while a LiveTip is open. Now called on its own from the view tick (when OpenSysTip is set).

* (210a) Diagnostics only: the rule 7 trace line from another section now says the pick's rank and, for the donor, work / best job rank (none = no job in the list yet) / teams / plan / seconds seen - to check why a team with work counted as free (Krivak ping-pong, (209)).

* (211) Debounce removed (DcAuto: `Debounce`, `Ship.Seen`). A section's work became a job only after 4 s of continuous work. Nothing needed it: without teams the work does not come and go by itself (holes stay, water does not go without pumping, a fire does not go out unattended unless water floods it, damage only grows), and what teams undo is held by their own job (holds / cover / keepOne / onSite); several effects of one hit are just planned in the next 2 s pass. What it did: a 4-6 s later reaction, and a hole - a section with work but no job in `needs` (rank none, plan 0) that the other rules took for free: rule 7 pulled such teams (probably the Krivak ping-pong (209)), and the gap filling needed a patch ("a fresh fire, still debounced, keeps its team" - dropped, `keepOne = ... && rj <= n.Rank`). The rule 7 diagnostics (210a) stay (without "seen").

* (212) System hint (DamageControlUi.Systems.cs, SystemTip): "being repaired" and the rate no longer come from the game. The game's DamageSummary.IsRepairing is set in BaseSystem.OnUpdate every 10 frames: on when the integrity grew since the last check, else off - so it flickers. Now: repaired = a team of the section works on systems (SysTeams) and this is the system RepairSystems takes (SysRepairFix.Target: the priority system, else the first that really needs repair, as the prefix chooses). Rate = the game's formula: teams on systems x Crew._systemRepair x SystemRepairSpeed (DcControl.SysRepairBase) x DCTeamEffectiveness x their mean health / fatigue efficiency x the fire penalty (FireWorkEff when a fire burns there). The "Level X in mm:ss" time follows from it (changes only with the teams / their efficiency). The measured rate (SysTrack) stays only as a fallback when the members are not found. The game's own icon (in the system list) still flickers - its cause is not known: user: no water to pump there (the pump / systems alternation guess was wrong). Diagnostics: opening a system's tooltip traces "System tip: section, integrity / max, teams on systems, repaired now: 'name' (this one), game flag, rate/s, fixed dt, time scale, float step at this integrity" - to see whether one physics step adds less than the float step (lost additions) or the repair goes to another system.

* (213) System tooltip refresh done as the other live tooltips: LiveTip got an overload for a tooltip with its own content (Border holder + Func<UIElement> make + an action on each opening); OpenTip holds Box / Make next to Tb / Text, UpdateOpenTips rebuilds Box.Child. The system row (the game's list row) gets it directly when the list's MouseMove first finds it (SysRow: holder + the summary the row shows now - a list may reuse a row), the opening also writes the "System tip" diagnostics line. The separate OpenSysTip / UpdateOpenSysTip of (208)/(210) are gone. Note: the trace that seemed to show no tooltip events (6527434a, no "System tip" lines) was made with build 20261104.2304, before (212) - it proved nothing.
* (214) Forecast card: "Critical" (red) lit at the start of every fight - one fire no team fights yet was enough (the Heavy condition for rest). ContactIntel.HeavyLevel: 2 = danger (sinking; flood stage 3+ with a leak; hull stage 3+ with fire / leak; fires 50%+), 1 = only an unfought fire, 0 = none; Heavy (rest, DcTeams) = level > 0 as before. Forecast: level 2 -> "Critical" red (sev 2), level 1 -> "Uncontrolled Fires" yellow (renamed from "Fire unattended" at the user's word, text 592) (sev 3, BWarnFc = CPastMuster). Localized (591 texts; also the (206) cfg description of ShowEnemyDamage, text 590, which had been left untranslated). READMEs / ChangeList updated.

* (215) The DC tab's card label "Forecast:" renamed "Status:" (text 593, localized); READMEs / ChangeLists say "Status card".

* (216) System hint: a system can stand in the lists of several sections (the Engine of Prj.1134 in sections 1, 2, 3 - trace 507e7464: teams of section 1 repaired it, the row of section 3 said "No damage control team in the section" while the integrity grew and the game's flag was on). Now every section that lists the system counts: repaired when the teams on systems of any of them take it first (SysRepairFix.Target); rate = sum over those sections, teams / mean efficiency over them; the line says "Teams repairing it (from other sections too)" when one of them is not the row's own section (text 594).

* (217) Storozhevoi (trace 507e7464, DC priority Fire): DC2 stayed plugging holes in section 2 (plan 0) while section 0 wanted a team for its fire ("0: 0->1 FH | 1: 1->1 | 2: 1->0 LW ... doing 1:F 2:L", unchanged for minutes). By the code only LeakRisk (a side under 19% with a weldable hull keeps its last team for any non-critical job, (193)) could hold it. User: the section's hull was at zero and the section fully flooded. LeakRisk exists so that an empty section does not take in water again through a reopened leak - a side that is full already (CurrentFloodingPercent >= 99.5, `FullSide`) cannot take in more, so it no longer counts (the team is free for the fire / other work by the plan) - only while that compartment's deck point is above the sea (SubmergedFlooding.DeckUnder; user: with a deck point under water the rule stays). Also affects the rest rule (DcTeams) and HullHoldsWater's fallback the same way.

* (218) Performance analysis (user: at 20x time compression the mod slows the game 1.5-2x). Static review, by likely weight: (1) DcControl.Red -> SubmergedFlooding.Margins without a cache, every physics step for every section a team may pump in (PumpGuard -> PumpSafe -> Score -> Red; thousands of quaternion steps per call, a lambda per TouchAngle; EdgeError caches per step, Red does not), plus ListOf / TrimOf by MethodInfo.Invoke; (2) the Repair* calls of DcControl go through MethodInfo.Invoke (the delegates fail: "delegate for Repair... failed ... using reflection"), object[] + boxing per call; (3) the CheckDamageControl prefix does reflection (teams property, AutoDC) and SectionTeams before the "no teams" exit, for 5 sections x every ship x every step; (4) DcTeams.Condition: new bool[5] and ContactIntel.Heavy (reflection over compartments) per step while a team is in the reserve, RestPull too; (5) LeaksHeldByFire / HullHoldsWater / LeakRisk / FireIn / PumpMoreWater: reflection per step per section; (6) the _currentFloodingValue getter postfix (patched although FloodImbalanceEffect is off); (7) FlameUpMul with FireGrowthLimit (_fScMaxSev.GetValue boxed), KnotsOf (2 CWT lookups per compartment per step), FireFoughtPostfix (GameNow by reflection); (8) DcAuto.Allocate every 2 game s (10/s per ship at 20x) with PumpGain -> Margins per wet section. Time compression sets Time.timeScale, so the physics steps per real second grow with it - everything per step x 20.
  Measurement first: src/Prof.cs ([General] ProfilePerformance, settings Debug tab "Hook times"; needs Debug for the trace). Slots with Stopwatch time and calls; every 10 s of real time a "Perf" block: frames, physics steps (Time.fixedTime changes), time compression, per slot calls (/step), ms, % of real time, mean us, sorted by time; '>' = part of another slot; a total of the top-level hooks. Hooks measured: ship tick (and its 5 parts), CheckDamageControl prefix, repair calls (DcControl.Work), PumpGuard, Score, Margins, allocation hook (+ count of full Allocate passes), _currentFloodingValue postfix, FloodingMul, FlameUpMul, WreckDrag, Sink postfix, hit postfixes, fire hooks, RepairSystems prefix, TimeCompressionFix.CoG, Heavy, map unit postfix, the DC view / EnemyStatus / DeckCorners per frame, leak checks. Wrappers (XxxCore) keep the original names for Harmony / transpilers. Off: one bool per hook.

* (219) Measurement (trace 5cead9c9, 11 ships): at 1x the mod took 16% of the real time, at 10x 35-44%; nearly all of it SubmergedFlooding.Margins (135-146 us a call, 10-16 calls a physics step) called from DcControl.Score (heel check of pumping / counterflooding, inside the repair calls: Score 5-10 a step, ~240 us each = Margins twice). Everything else together ~1-3%. Fix: Margins keeps its last result per ship (State.Mc*) and returns it while the list and trim moved less than 0.1 degree and for at most 0.5 s (Time.fixedTime; the draft changes slowly); the hint values (s.M) are made again from it. Expected: Margins a few times a second per ship instead of every step.

* (220) Measurement after (219) (trace 6f5eccfa): 1x 16% -> 2.7% of real time, 10x 35-44% -> ~20%, physics steps at 10x 272/s -> 541-595/s (the game keeps up twice as well); Margins ~2-4 us a call (cached). Left at 10x: CheckDamageControl prefix 11% (Score without Margins 5.5%: ListOf / TrimOf by MethodInfo.Invoke and LevelGauge over all compartments, 5-10 times a step for the same ship; the Repair* calls by MethodInfo.Invoke), ship tick 5%, allocation hook 1.5%. Done:
  - DcControl.Caller: Repair* by a DynamicMethod ((object, int) -> castclass + call; on an InvalidCast / Verification / MethodAccess error it falls back to Invoke for good; "fast call of ... failed" in the trace if it cannot be made).
