  - CheckDamageControl prefix: teams / AutoDC by fast getters (Prop<T>), and a section with no team returns at once (the game does nothing there either; a task given by hand that waits for its team is no longer dropped by an empty section).
  - FireIn by fast getters; Score's one-off buffers (PumpGain, LeakGain, CounterScore, PumpGuard, PumpSafe) reused instead of new arrays; DcTeams.Condition reuses its array.
  - SectionEffects.Tick: fast getters / setters for the compartments' leak rate, water %, fire severity / max / growth, arrays, count.
  - Game time by StaticNum in DcAuto (allocation hook every step) and AdmPlugin.GameNow (FireFoughtPostfix).

* (221) Release 1.7.0 prepared again: ChangeList.txt / ChangeList-1000.txt (net change since 1.6.0: Status card, Uncontrolled Fires, Auto DC reacts at once, system / team tooltips, own-ship map icons, high time compression load); READMEs: the system tooltip line; then all three condensed under 8000 characters (Steam limit; ~6300 now), same content in shorter words. The AutoCounterflood description corrected (red zone / half of it, letting water in only - it still said 40% / 20% and pumping), text 598 localized. Release dll and lang rebuilt. Not committed (waiting for the user's command).

* (222) Damage from collisions between ships ([HullIntegrity] CollisionDamage, CollisionMinSpeed; src/Collisions.cs). The game gives ships only physics for a ship-ship contact: Vessel/Submarine.OnCollisionEnter looks at "Terrain" and nothing else (hull colliders are convex MeshColliders on the ship's Rigidbody, mass = displacement / 40, 1 unit = 67.2 m). User: a ram in the game is a lasting process - the bow climbs onto the victim and may slide over it - so the first blow is not the damage. Model: the energy the physics wears down while the contact lasts, step by step. Hooks: postfix of Vessel/Submarine.OnCollisionEnter (first step, and it adds a CollisionRelay to the ship's GameObject) + CollisionRelay.OnCollisionStay (every later physics step; the game's classes have no OnCollisionStay, a Harmony patch cannot add a method). Per step: Collision.impulse J and relativeVelocity v (pre-solve), reduced mass mu (tons = rb.mass * 40): E = J_n*v_n - J_n^2/(2 mu) along the mean contact normal + the same for the sliding direction (friction), each >= 0 (checked on a toy: one inelastic step, 5 or 50 steps all sum to the ideal mu*v^2/2; an elastic bounce 0; a ship resting on another has v = 0, so 0). The ship takes share = m_other / (m_self + m_other) of it: fraction of one compartment = Strength * share * E / (Toughness * M_self), Toughness = 10 kJ/t (a constant; for one blow = v^2 / 20 * share^2: equal ships 10 m/s = 1.25 compartments each; 500 t boat on 8000 t at 15 kn = 2.6 for the boat, 0.01 for the ship). Part of the work lifts the bow while it climbs (gravity), which the formula counts as lost: Strength is the knob. Points = fraction * the game's compartment integrity (HullState.OriginalX, before the mod's multiplier: a stronger hull loses less of itself). Where: the contact points of THIS step -> ship frame (Compartments._compartmentsTransform) -> section by the nearest compartment object z, side by the sign of x (port <= 0); damage split by the number of contacts per compartment, so it moves along the hull as the bow climbs; what a destroyed compartment cannot take spills 50% to the neighbour towards the middle. Leak: per contact (Episode, per hull, new one after 1.5 game s without a step) the damage a compartment took is summed (x1 at the waterline = within 3 m above the sea, over it x0.25 under 50% / x0.5 under 25% integrity / 0 above) and the leak is RAISED to sum / game integrity * HullFloodingVulnerability * Globals._floodingRateMultiplier (IncreaceFloodingRate only when the compartment's _floodingRate is below that: it sets it; calling it every step would add rate/2 each time). Guards: nothing in the first 5 s of a mission (ships placed overlapping), relative speed under CollisionMinSpeed (1 kn) = nothing, at most 6 compartments' worth per contact, destroyed ships take nothing, others than ships (terrain, aircraft) ignored. Not touched: the sinking limit (_limitIntegrity belongs to blast hits), fires, DC teams' wounds, grounding on the terrain. HitLog.AddCollision: one "Collision" record per contact in the ship's hit list (tooltip) and one hull-loss log entry that the lasting contact adds to. Trace (Debug): "Collision: A with B starts / , N s: relative speed, masses, kJ so far = F compartments' worth, hull lost; this step P2:dmg/leak r" - at the start and every 2 s.
  BUILD: CollisionRelay.OnCollisionStay(Collision) needs the type at compile time: the csproj now references UnityEngine.PhysicsModule.dll from ManagedDir (the game's own Managed folder has it). This sandbox's Managed folder did not, so a one-class stub assembly UnityEngine.PhysicsModule.dll (namespace UnityEngine { class Collision }, version 0.0.0.0) sits in /home/claude/managed/Managed - build only, never shipped; everything else about Collision / ContactPoint / Rigidbody stays reflection.
  Profiles: Advanced 1.0, Base Game 0.0 (the game has none). 8 texts localized (606 in all). READMEs: item "Collisions between ships". NOT verified in game: that ships collide at all in the physics matrix (hulls are non-trigger convex colliders, the game's own OnCollisionEnter exists, so they should), that a component added during OnCollisionEnter gets OnCollisionStay from the next step, that Collision.impulse is filled in Stay. If a ship's trace has no "Collision:" line when ships touch, look at those. ChangeList files not changed (no new version yet).

* (223) Collisions, first test (trace 03a6ff84 + screenshot: the Storozhevoi's red bow stuck against a cruiser's side, speed -1.2 kn): the trace has the two "patched ...OnCollisionEnter" lines and NOT ONE "Collision" line - no damage line, no exception, so either the game never called Vessel.OnCollisionEnter for the ship-ship contact (the hull colliders coll_hull* are "active False" in the Deck corners trace: the physical collider of a ship may be another one, or on a kinematic body that is not the Vessel's Rigidbody), or the postfix returned silently (strength 0, first 5 s, relative speed under 1 kn). The only sign of damage: "CapsizeFix: the ship starts to sink" at 21:33:48 (which ship is not in the line). Diagnostics added (Debug trace only): the "settings:" line ends with "collisions <strength> (slowest <kn> kn)"; Collisions.Skip writes, once in 3 s per ship and reason, "Collisions: <ship>: OnCollisionEnter, other collider '<name>' of '<root>', rigidbody '<name>'/none" for every Enter, and the reason of every early return (not a ship, no rigidbody, destroyed, first 5 s, relative speed under the limit, no contact points, no energy lost with J / v). Next: repeat the ram with Debug on; no "OnCollisionEnter" line at all = the game's collider does not reach the Vessel (then a different hook: the collider's own body / a Physics query).
* (224) Own profile lost its [Profile] Parent: Profile.CurrentValues() (used by the settings window to save / Save as / Commit) built a Profile with Parent = null, and Copy() keeps Parent for a non-built-in profile, so saving the profile of the player wrote the file without the [Profile] section. Keys added later (CollisionDamage) then came from Base Game (0 = off) instead of the profile it was made from. CurrentValues() now reads the Parent from the active own profile file; a file without [Profile] (parent lost) is read as made from Advanced (LoadProfile and CurrentValues), so the next save writes "Parent = Advanced".
* (225) Trace 7c6501ea (ram at about 15 kn): OnCollisionEnter DOES fire for ship against ship (coll_collider of the other ship), but Unity reports a relative speed of 0.09 across / 0.62 along m/s (0.22 kn) and impulse 0 - the Rigidbody velocities do not carry the ships' real speed, so the model sees no energy. Conversions to metres and knots were checked and are right. Debug now writes a "Collisions: ... motion:" line at the first contact: the game speed (getVelocityInKnots) of both ships, each Rigidbody velocity and isKinematic, Unity relative speed and impulse. Next: if the game speed is ~15 kn and the Rigidbody is ~0 / kinematic, take the relative velocity from the game speed and heading (energy 0.5*mu*v_n^2 spent over the contact, no solver impulse).
* (226) Trace 34e0b6a2 (ram at 20.5 kn): motion line = game speed 20.5 / 2.7 kn, Rigidbody 0.34 / 0.25 kn, Unity relative speed 0.56 kn, impulse 0. The game moves its ships itself, the physics sees almost nothing. Collisions.StepCore now takes the relative velocity from the game speeds (SpeedEffect.KnotsOf) along each ship's transform.forward when that is more than twice Unity's (then the impulse is ignored), and spends the contact's energy as a budget: 0.5 * mu * (v_across^2 + 0.15 * v_along^2) at the start of the contact, worn down with a time constant of 1 s (Episode.Budget; BudgetTime, TangentShare). A ship going astern is taken as going ahead (the game gives |speed|). Unity's own impulse model stays for when the solver does see the speeds. Constants are a first guess, to be set from a test.
* (227) Trace 10033384 (Sverdlov 17.4 kn, Storozhevoi 7.4 kn, relative 20.3 kn by the game speeds and headings): the game-speed model works for the first step (693 kJ, 0.06 compartments, 1.5 points, leak 0.059 on S4 of the Petya; the Sverdlov 0.00), but NO further step of the contact was logged: either OnCollisionStay never reaches CollisionRelay, or the contact lasts one physics step (the ships are moved by the game, not pushed apart). The budget (~35000 kJ here, ~3 compartments for the Petya) is therefore almost unspent. The trace now logs the first four steps of a contact ("step N") and then every 0.5 s (LogEvery 2.0 -> 0.5). Next: if no Stay comes, spend the budget from the Enter step alone (over BudgetTime, by a timer) instead of waiting for Stay.
* (228) The contact budget is now spent by a timer, not by OnCollisionStay (trace 10033384: after the Enter step nothing came, 2% of the budget used, 0.06 compartments). Collisions.StepCore keeps the measuring and calls Apply(...) (the damage of one step: sections by the contact points, spill, leak, hit log, trace); the Episode remembers the last contact (points, masses, relative velocity, other ship, Budget0); CollisionRelay.FixedUpdate calls Collisions.Tick, which spends Budget * (1 - exp(-dt / BudgetTime)) per step for 5 * BudgetTime (5 s), stops at 1% of the budget, a destroyed ship, or MaxFraction. Stay and Tick share Episode.Last, so a step is never spent twice. The budget of the trace case is ~35000 kJ = ~3 compartments for the 1110 t ship (about 50 times the first step), so the strength factors were NOT changed.
* (229) Collisions rebuilt on the game's own speeds, with braking, a bow factor and a Collisions tab. The game moves a Vessel itself (ObjectBase._velocityInKnots, signed, integrated towards the ordered speed each fixed step, then transform.Translate along the heading), so ObjectBase.setVelocityInKnots(float) changes the real speed and the engines speed the ship up again. Collisions.cs: Pair (shared by the two ships of a contact; A = the ship whose step came first, N = horizontal normal from B to A, flipped by the line between the centres) and Advance(pair, now), once per step: the relative velocity from both ships' signed speeds along their headings; target = across: -Restitution(0.05) * closing, along: (1 - kappa) * sliding, kappa = GripScrape(0.15) + GripPress(0.75) * closing / speed (max 0.9); the change is spread over BrakeTime (0.7 s); impulse = mu * the change of the relative velocity, each ship gets its part along its own heading, times [Collisions] Braking; the player ship is always slowed, AI ships when BrakeAI; E = 0.5 * mu * (v^2 - v'^2) (kJ) goes to the hulls: Toughness_i = 10 kJ/t * (1 + (BowStrength - 1) * a^2), a = |normal . heading| (bow/stern 1, side 0), BowStrength 4. The contact lasts while the steps come, at least ForceTime 3 s from its start (Tick from CollisionRelay.FixedUpdate), at most 20 s; it ends by itself when the relative speed falls under CollisionMinSpeed. Contact points are kept in each ship's own frame (Episode.Loc / WY): the ships move on. PushApart (default off) moves the pair apart at 0.5 m/s along the normal, the lighter ship more. The budget / timer model of (228) is gone. Settings: new tab Collisions (the two earlier keys of [HullIntegrity] moved there in the window only, files unchanged) and section [Collisions]: BowStrength, Braking, BrakeAI, GripScrape, GripPress, PushApart; Advanced.cfg: braking 1, Base Game.cfg: braking 0. Not done: yaw from an oblique blow; lateral push of the struck ship (the game's movement is along the heading only). Unverified in the game.
* (230) Collisions tab: master switch [Collisions] Enabled (default true; Base Game.cfg false) - the other settings of the tab show only while it is on (ShownWhen); Collisions.On gates the hooks, the relay and Tick (Strength only scales the hull damage now, braking works with Strength 0). BrakeAI replaced by [Collisions] AIShips (default true): when off, a ship that the player does not control takes no damage and is not slowed; two AI ships: nothing. Labels: "Minimum collision speed", "Scraping speed loss" / "Pressing speed loss" (they are speed lost, not damage), group "On Contact". Unit texts shortened (the comment column is narrow); the explanations are in the tooltips (Braking: 1.0 = the full loss of speed of such a blow, 0.5 = half).
* (231) [Collisions] was not in AdmPlugin.ProfileSections, so WriteProfileFile left the section out of the player's profile and Apply lost the tab's settings (the profile was re-read without them, the keys came back from the parent): added. Enabled now gates only the hull damage (it hides Collision damage and Bow strength; Collisions.DamageOn); the hooks run while Collisions.Active = DamageOn || Braking > 0, the rest of the tab (minimum speed, AI ships, braking, drags, push apart) is always shown.
* (232) Trace 131552ad (Sverdlov 18.6 kn into the Storozhevoi 1.7 kn): the frigate was wrecked (6 compartments cap), the cruiser lost 0.6-2 points, nobody was pushed away. Causes: (1) the game moves a ship along its heading only, so the sideways part of the impulse was lost and the closing speed never fell (287 MJ in 2.6 s against 48 MJ for the blow); (2) the damage share m_other / (m_self + m_other) per ton of the ship itself made the cruiser take ~1/800 of the frigate's. Now: Slip (per ship) keeps the sideways speed the impulse gives (SlipStep in Tick moves the ship, decay SlipTime 2 s), it is part of the velocities in Advance, so the struck ship is carried away and the closing speed falls (frigate 4 m/s sideways after 0.5 s, about 8 m moved in all); Pair.Cap = 0.5 * mu * v^2 at the start bounds the energy of a blow (also with Braking 0); damage fraction of a compartment = Strength * heavy * zone * E / (Toughness * M_lighter), heavy = M_heavier / (M_a + M_b), zone = 1 / (1 + (BowStrength - 1) * a^2): the same part of a compartment for both hulls, the bow 1 / BowStrength of the side (a frigate 4 compartments, the cruiser bow 1 at 18 kn, K 4; equal ships 1.25 each as before). Simulated in Python, not run in the game. PushApart is a separate small separation and is off by default.
* (233) Trace aa9ad671: both hulls damaged (frigate 2.7, cruiser bow 0.76 compartments, ratio 3.6), the frigate shoved 4.3 m/s sideways, but the cruiser lost only 0.5 kn and the leak was 0.18 on the frigate / 0 on the cruiser. Water resistance: motion uses effective masses, m * (1 + AddedAlong(0.1) * a^2 + AddedAcross(4) * WaterResistance * (1 - a^2)), a = |normal . heading|: the impulse mu_eff * change of the relative velocity is divided by each ship's effective mass, the hull energy keeps the real masses (so the cap 0.5 * mu * v^2 is unchanged); across its hull a ship counts ~5 times heavier, so a ram into a side is braked more (cruiser: 25% of the closing speed against 6%) and shoves its target less. SlipTime 2 -> 1.5 s. New setting [Collisions] WaterResistance (default 1.0). Leaks: overFactor = max(old, clamp((Fraction - 0.1) / 0.4)) - a ship that has taken half a compartment in a contact leaks as at the waterline, a weak blow (under 0.1) only by the old rules. Yaw of the struck ship is not modelled (its resistance is in the added mass).

(234) Auto DC and a flooded engine: the engine lies in several sections and SectionEffects holds its systems down while any of them is fully flooded (>75%), so repair through an unflooded section is useless. DcAuto computes engineWet (a section holding the engine is Locked): no priority mark/job for the engine, no engineMode ("every team goes to the engine repair"), no ordinary Systems job for the engine; ship.EngineWet change is traced; repair resumes when the water is pumped out. Settings window: "Push ships apart" moved under the Collisions group; the Collision damage tooltip shortened (did not fit the window).

(235) Collisions tuning: GripScrape removed (kappa = GripPress * closing / |rel|, so parallel hulls scrape for free; GripPress default 0.75 -> 0.1). Braking counts twice (BrakeGain 2, each ship's share of the change capped at 1 so it never overshoots the relative change) and AddedAcross 4 -> 8 (water resistance twice). Defaults BowStrength 5, CollisionMinSpeed 3 (new profile values too). New [Collisions] Flooding (default 1, shown after Collision damage, hidden with Enabled off): multiplies the leak a collision opens (target rate in Apply). Texts of the new setting are English-only until the next loc pass.

(236) Release 1.8.0 prepared: version in csproj / AdmPlugin.PluginVersion / PUBLISHING / READMEs; ChangeList.txt and ChangeList-1000.txt (net change since 1.7.0: collisions, Auto DC and a flooded engine); the 5 new texts (rows 648-652 of en.tsv) translated into the 8 languages, Release/lang regenerated (rows 600, 607, 610, 611 and other stale ones are "missing" by design).

(237) Performance review of the new code. Found and fixed: (1) Collisions.Postfix built two diagnostic strings (Describe, Motion: reflection, GetComponent, root.GetComponentInChildren) on EVERY Vessel/Submarine OnCollisionEnter, before it was known the other body is a ship (terrain, shells, debris) - removed from the Enter path (the "Collision: ... starts" lines stay); (2) CollisionRelay.FixedUpdate (every physics step for every ship that ever had a contact) did a reflection property read of Compartments and GameNow twice - the Compartments is cached in the relay, GameNow read once; (3) Skip keyed its throttle with NameOf (reflection) on every call - now the instance hash. Checked and left: Advance/Apply run only during a contact (a few seconds, allocation of a few small arrays per step); DcAuto engineWet is one dictionary lookup per section (Locked) and touches the systems lists only for a fully flooded section.

(238) BrakeGain 2 -> 4 (Slowing down counts four times; each ship's share still capped at 1), AddedAcross 8 -> 4 (Water resistance back to its first strength).

(239) (238) corrected: BrakeGain back to 2 (Slowing down counts twice, as asked), AddedAcross stays 4 (Water resistance back to its first strength).

(240) Defaults: [Collisions] AIShips false, PushApart true (code defaults and both built-in profiles).

(241) Fire growth and flooding depend on the ship's displacement. SpeedEffect.SizeFactorOf(tons): 1 from 1000 t up, rising smoothly along ln(1000/tons)/ln(20) to [FireAndFlooding] SmallShipSpeedUp (default 3; Base Game.cfg 1 = off) at 50 t, constant below; the ship's Compartments._displacement is read once per ship (Owner.Size). SpeedEffect.Flood / Fire (used by the flooding and fire transpilers and by the tooltips) multiply by it, so the tooltips and Auto DC see the same rates. Settings: Fire & Flooding tab, group "Ship size: effect on flooding and fire", "Small ships: faster" (mod only, no game value). 4 new texts (rows 653-656), 8 languages. README / ChangeLists: one clause / line. After the container was replaced the working tree was restored from the last delivered zip (240) and the toolchain rebuilt (dotnet 8 from apt, BepInEx 5.4.23.2 core from GitHub, a stub AnchorChain.dll at /home/claude/deps/ac - build only).

(242) Hit list: secondary explosions of ship systems. BaseSystem.ExplodeSystem (game) builds a Blastzone with an AmmunitionParameters that has no name and _isSecondaryExplosion, hits its own ship with it (the game's own log says "Secondary explosion!") - the hit list showed it as "?   (explosion)". Now ExplodeSystem prefix/postfix (AdmPlugin.ExplodePreHook / ExplodePostHook -> HitLog.ExplodePre / ExplodePost): while it runs, the records of its blasts are named "Explosion of <system's DisplayName>" (text 657, 8 languages); if no blast reached the exploding ship's own log, the postfix adds one record for the section whose _listOfSystems holds the system (side from the position of the mount / first collider in the ship's frame). Not seen in a game yet - check a trace of a magazine / mount explosion. Build tools for the container: ilspycmd (dotnet tool, DOTNET_ROLL_FORWARD=Major) -> decompiled game source at /root/gsrc/all.

(243) Systems out by flooding, new rule (SectionEffects.Tick): a section is "flooded" (Full, so Locked: systems brought down to 19%, inoperable, no repair, Auto DC skips it) when ONE of its two compartments holds 75% of water or more (FullLevel, was ">75% of the mean") OR the section as a whole (the mean of its sides) holds 50% or more (FullSectionLevel). >= both. The fire-decay limit still uses the mean. Text of [FireAndFlooding] FullFloodSystems rewritten (row 658, 8 languages). The hit list: the "Hits on the ship: N" tooltip of the DC tab IS what (242) changed (HitLogTip shows the records). Fixed in the same pass: nothing else.

(244) The previous zip did not start in the game: "Could not resolve type ... expected class 'AnchorChain.ACPluginAttribute'". The build stub of AnchorChain.dll (made after the container was replaced, the real one was lost) guessed the attribute as ACPluginAttribute; the real one is AnchorChain.ACPlugin (ctor string, string, string, string[] = null, string[] = null) and IAnchorChainMod. Stub fixed, the custom attribute in the new dll is byte for byte the old one's (checked with ilspycmd -il against the 1.7.0 build). Lesson: the dll of a build made with a stub reference must be checked against an older dll (IL of the attributes / references) before sending. The stub is for building only (/home/claude/anchor_stub).

## Game 0.9.0 (2026-11-11/12): compatibility check against 0.8.7 (static, decompiled both Seapower-Scripts.dll; not run in the game)
Builds without errors. The damage core is byte-identical to 0.8.7: `Blastzone`, `Compartments`, `FloodingCompartment`, `SystemCompartment`, `BaseSystem`, `Ship`, `DM` (so every transpiler / prefix / postfix of ADM on them keeps matching). Changed classes ADM touches by name: only `ObjectBase` (Save/LoadStateFromFile, not patched by us; our save hooks are on Compartments/BaseSystem), `UnitStatusViewModel` (SwitchUnit: a Supplies tab was added, `UnitStatusTab.Supplies = 5`; DeckCorners / EnemyStatus patch SwitchUnit, still matches), `MapUnitViewModel.Update` (ContactIntel, one extra land-unit line), `IniHandler`/`Utils.openPersistentIniFile` (AnchorChainEntry: signatures unchanged), `GameTime` (new RadioSequenceForcesNormalTime / ForceLowFrameRateTimestep; TimeCompressionFix only reads `fixedDeltaTime` and patches Compartments.OnFixedUpdate, unaffected). Game data: vessel inis only got `[Measured]` (baked areas/volume), `DamagedMesh`/`HideWithActiveDamageModel`, `ArmorType=None` on rafts; no damage parameter changed. No ADM change needed. Still to check in the game: the Unit Status tab row with the new Supplies tab and our Damage Control tab/overlay.

## Settings window: "profile: x", reset to the profile (2026-11-12)
`GameText(f)` = `GameTextCore` (the old "default / game" lines) + `ProfileLine(f)` (the value of `_from`, the stored profile the work came from; same wording rules as `GameLine`, no line for global options and for the Speed table). The row's ↺ takes `_from`'s value for profile settings (so the dot `Differs`, the status "↺ = back to it" and the button agree), the default for global options. In Base Game the profile line equals the game line (both are that profile). New texts: `profile: {0}`, `no limit`, `instant`, `Back to the profile's value` (rows 659-662, all 8 languages). Tab order: Collisions just before Debug, Debug always last (no code depends on tab indexes).

## Debug trace additions (2026-11-12)
`HitLog.Postfix` writes `hit <ammo>: section N port|stbd[, stopped by armour][, explosion][, dud], damage D -> integrity I%, leak R` when Debug is on (`ReserveHits.Describe`: integrity and `_floodingRate` of the hit compartment after the game's processing; `HitLog.Trace` was never wired before, now it is). At start `NoteForeignHitPatches` logs every other Harmony owner with a prefix on `Blastzone.OnHitUnit` (SeaPowerArmorSim skips the game's hit processing for its custom-armour ships, so ADM's hit list, leaks, fire of hits and DC wounds do not see those hits). Stale rows 607, 610, 611 of en.tsv (old Collisions descriptions) are untranslated and unused; left in place to keep the numbering.

`tools/loc_extract.py` labels the rows of `en.tsv` kept only for the numbering: `obsolete` = the text is no longer in `src/*.cs` (39 rows, among them 607, 610, 611; no translation needed, nothing reads them), `old` = still used but not found by the extractor (runtime texts such as "on", "fire"). Column 3 is informational only (`loc_gen.py` ignores it).

"default: x" and "profile: x" are never shown together: `GameText` passes `withDefault = (ProfileLine == null)` to `GameTextCore`, so profile settings show "game: x" + "profile: x" (or "game: no analogue" + "profile: x"), global options and the Speed table keep "default: x" (+ "game: x").

(244) Hit list marks duds. HitLog.Postfix reads the private Blastzone.WarheadMalfunction (= _weapon._warheadMalfunction, set in Blastzone.CreateExplosion when OptionsManager.WeaponFailuresEnabled and random > _ap._warheadReliability; then GetWeaponDamage uses the reduced _warheadDudPower) via reflection (HitLog.IsDud, PropertyInfo cached in Init) into Rec.Dud; saved as flag 8; tooltip "(dud)" (row 663, 8 languages); trace ", dud". Only ammo that can fail (missiles, torpedoes, RBU, bombs, aerial rockets) and only with Weapon Failures on.

(245) HullFireVulnerability auto-compensated: FireIntegrity/FireIntegrityFc divide by (MultOf * HullFireVulnerability) (like flooding), IncendiaryFrac = share * HullFireVulnerability (no /m). 1.0 = a hit starts a fire of the same % of the compartment as in the game, whatever the hull multiplier. Before: 1.0 = fire m times weaker; profiles that set HullFireVulnerability = Warships to compensate by hand must go back to 1.0. Text row 664 (8 languages).

(246) Release 1.9.0 prepared (1.8.0 was published with collisions, Auto DC engine fix only): version in csproj / AdmPlugin.PluginVersion / PUBLISHING / READMEs; ChangeList.txt and ChangeList-1000.txt list only what is new since the published 1.8.0 (0.9.0 only, profile line, debug trace, small ships, hit list explosions and duds, fire vulnerability, flooded-section rule).

(247) SettingsHub.Install: R.A.T.S. Keel starts mods after the start-up load screen is over, so the ExecuteLoadScreen postfix never fires and the "Alternative Mods" button was missing. Now LoadScreenIsDone(game) (Singleton<LoadScreen>.InstanceExists(false) -> Instance.LoadText == "Done") -> DoInstall at once + MainMenuCtorPostfix for an already built menu. Same edit as in the canonical copy (ASE, hub-keel-fix-instructions.md); to be replaced by the ASE copy as is. Check under Keel: log line "load screen is already over: menu entries are installed now".

(248) Keel warned that ADM looks up types by name that 0.9.1 lacks. DcTeams: SeaPower.BaseObject -> SeaPower.ObjectBase (IsDestroyed is a public property of ObjectBase; with null the "ship destroyed" check of the DC teams always returned false). SysPriority: DamageSummary is SeapowerUI.ViewModels.DamageSummary (the name-scan fallback stays). All other GetType("SeaPower...") / ("SeapowerUI...") names were checked against Seapower-Scripts.dll of 0.9.1 (SensorSystem+SensorTypeSet and Singleton`1 exist as nested / generic types). SettingsHub.cs differs from the canonical (ASE) only in the order of _harmony / _installed. Check in game: no DC team goes to a destroyed ship; no ADM warning in the Keel report (Ctrl+Shift+K).

(249) src/SettingsHub.cs replaced as is by the canonical copy (ASE eb6609e): Register calls RefreshLabels, so the text of the menu buttons already built (main menu button, pause menu buttons) follows the mods registered later (Keel starts the mods one after another): "Alternative Mods" when several are active, the mod's name when one. The Keel branch (LoadScreenIsDone) is in it. Release/_info.ini: ApproximateVersion=0.9.0 -> GreaterThanEqualToVersion=0.9.0 (as ASE, AWC, AGI: no compatibility warning in the game). Check: main and pause menu show "Alternative Mods" with several mods active, the mod's name with one.

(250) SettingsHub.Install now calls RefreshLabels() right after _game is set (src/SettingsHub.cs = the canonical copy again, as is). Cause of the "Game Interface" label that stayed in the main menu: every mod dll has its OWN copy of the SettingsHub class with its own statics; a mod calls Register(...) and then Install(...), so at Register time _game of this copy is still null and RefreshLabels returned at once; the button put in by the copy of a mod that started earlier (AGI, StartEarly) was never refreshed. RefreshLabels stays in Register too. Check under Keel with AGI (StartEarly) and the other mods: the main menu shows "Alternative Mods".

(251) Fires from hits stopped by the main armour: [FireAndFlooding] BlockedHitFireChance (Factor, code default 0.5; Base Game profile 0.0 = off, Advanced 0.4; one entry = the switch and the multiplier; GUI: tab Fire, group Fire, "Fire from hits stopped by armour", "x the chance of a shell that gets through - 0 = off", step 0.05, 0..3; ModOnly + NoGame). Game: in Blastzone.ProcessCompartmentHit everything about fire and flooding sits in "if (!blockedByMainArmor)". The fire roll there (num8 = 20 for a Projectile x Random(0.6, 0.9); the player ship minus Globals._catchFireChanceReduction, else (_weapon != null ? a player weapon plus _causeFireChanceBonus : a Projectile of the player taskforce plus the bonus); num8 > Globals._rnd.Next(0, 101)) is made only for a compartment other than _hitCompartmentIndex; the hit compartment itself is rolled in OnHitUnit (first impact, armour or not, so a blocked shell could always light the hit compartment). So the option adds the roll for the compartments around the hit one, not a second roll for the hit one. src/BlockedFire.cs: BlockedFire.Prefix is called from SpillPrefix (the existing prefix of ProcessCompartmentHit). Conditions: blockedByMainArmor, not _isSecondaryExplosion, _weaponType == Projectile, the collider flooding_<side>_<n> with n != hit index, ADM model on. Chance as the game (same RNG) x option. The fire is the shell of a penetrating hit on a neighbour: damage x Random(0.6, 0.9) (what the game does to a secondary compartment), severity 0, growth = damage x 0.5 / ref / 20 / 2, ref = AdmPlugin.FireIntegrityFc(fc, fc._maxIntegrity) (hull multiplier and HullFireVulnerability are applied as for every other hit; the chance is not changed by HullFireVulnerability); SystemCompartment.AddCompartmentFire is called on _systemCompartments[n-1] of the flooding compartment, so FlameUp, size and speed multipliers and the fire log work as usual. Nothing else: no integrity loss, no flooding, no team wounds. Hit list: HitLog.Rec.ArmFire (save flag 16); the neighbour is processed in another call than the hit compartment, before or after it, so HitLog keeps a per-Blastzone note (ConditionalWeakTable, reset when the hit unit changes) that waits for the record or marks the last one. Tooltip: "(stopped by armour, started a fire)" (rows 666-668); debug trace: "blocked hit lights a fire: section N, chance C %, growth +G %" and ", fire from the blocked hit" in the hit line when known. Texts: rows 665-668, 8 languages. Not seen in a game yet.

(252) [FireAndFlooding] GunFireSeverity (Factor, code default 0.5; Base Game 0.0, Advanced 0.5; GUI: tab Fire, "Fires from a gun shell hit", "x as for bombs and missiles - 0 = off", step 0.05, 0..3; ModOnly + NoGame): a gun shell that lights a fire also gives it a starting severity. Game: a Projectile starts a fire with AddCompartmentFire(0, growth): OnHitUnit (hit compartment) severity num10/2 is zeroed for a Projectile, growth num10/20, num10 = GetWeaponDamage / Compartments._maxIntegrity; ProcessCompartmentHit (neighbour) num10 = damage x 0.5 / fc._maxIntegrity, severity 0, growth num10/20/2 = damage/integrity/80. A bomb / missile / rocket adds severity num10/2 and growth num10/20 (no halving). src/GunFire.cs: the starting severity is put back as Start x growth x 10 (hit compartment) or x 40 (neighbour: damage/integrity/2). Where the game calls AddCompartmentFire(0, growth) from is known by a ThreadStatic mode: prefix of OnHitUnit (GunHitPre: mode 1 for a non-secondary Projectile, with Time.frameCount as a guard against a stale mode), SpillPrefix / ReserveHitPostfix (mode 2 inside ProcessCompartmentHit and back to 1), OnHitUnit postfix (0). AddFirePrefix (the existing prefix of SystemCompartment.AddCompartmentFire) takes the relativeSeverity by ref and sets it when it is 0 and the growth is > 0. A call with its own severity (_addFire incendiary 0.125 / 0.25 / 0.5) is left alone. The growth already carries ADM integrity (hull multiplier, HullFireVulnerability), so the severity does too. BlockedFire passes GunFire.Neighbour(growth) = Start x growth x 40 itself. Texts: rows 669-671, 8 languages. Not seen in a game yet; check the fire 0f a section right after a shell hit (Fire log: Debug + LogFires shows "hit (+x% severity ...").

- Diagnostics (wound funnel): in the trace a `teams:` line (health, any integrity, reserve hits, wound chance, fire wound) follows the settings, and `ReserveHits.Postfix` traces the first 3 times for every reason a hit does not reach the teams ("ReserveHits: hit skipped - ..."), and "passed to the teams" when it does. Reason: a battle trace had 43 penetrating hits and not a single "Hit on section" / wound line with all team-health options on.
- Fix: `ObjectBase._taskforce` is a property in 0.9.0 and 0.9.1 (checked on both game DLLs), but `ReserveHits.Init` asked for a field -> "members missing" and the whole hit->team funnel (wounds of DC teams in sections and in the reserve) was dead, as was the side of a unit in `ContactIntel`. Now `ReserveHits.TaskforceMember/TaskforceOf` accept either. `Blastzone._taskforce` is a field (BlockedFire). Compatibility check 0.9.0 vs 0.9.1 (GameFiles 1096233 vs 7a9eaba): all 235 member names ADM reflects on keep their kind (field / property / method) and all 22 types exist; method bodies / IL patterns were not compared (the trace shows every patch applied on 0.9.1).
- BepInEx log only for start-up diagnostics and errors: LogFires (every fire started by a hit: +severity, +growth in % of the maximum, and every stage change) and LogShipIntegrity now write to the trace file (`Diag`, also with Debug off); runtime status lines (profile in use, mod on/off, settings re-read, hull changes) go to the trace. Descriptions translated (rows 674, 675).
- The trace file has every line (also those of the BepInEx log: `Say` / `Warn` write to both; lines from before the trace file is known are buffered and written first); the BepInEx log keeps only start-up diagnostics and errors. `Diag` lines (LogFires, LogShipIntegrity) go to the trace only.
- The wound table (tooltip of "Wound chance"): two ships (ijn_bb_kongo, rm_bb_roma) and five shells (rm_cal_90mm, rm_cal_152mmhe, rm_cal_152mm, ijn_cal_356mm, rm_cal_381mmsap) of a test battle added to WantShips / WantAmmo; whatever the game does not have is skipped with a line in the trace.
- Wound formula (changed twice in 1.9.0, last version): chance = power / 12 / sqrt(health) x WoundChance (was power / 6 until the trace 653aba92: Roma/Kongo lost all teams in a minute; the halving is in the formula, WoundChance stays 1) (was 5.4% x power^1.83 / health^1.18); the mean wound = 0.2 x power^0.6 / health^0.3 x HitWound, at most the whole (was power x strength / health with the strength up to 2q); the wound itself is uniform from (2 x mean - 1) to 2 x mean, clipped to 0..1. Why: on big ships (health 100) a 356 mm hit wounded a team in 8.5% of cases by 6% of its health on average; the aim: 90 mm HE on a big ship about 5% / 10%, 356 mm about 42% / 36%, 381 mm 53% / 41%. Small ships become more vulnerable too. The table in the tooltip of "Wound chance" shows it.
- Teams on their way (St.Moving) can be wounded now: a hit on a section also rolls the teams passing that section at that moment (`SectionOnTheWay`: the straight line from where the team left to where it goes, the reserve = the central section; the pick is among working + passing teams). A wounded walker goes to the reserve from that section (no section count to correct: a walker is in none). Trace: "teams there N + M on the way", "(a team on the way)".
- Wound table colours: up to 30% the old log-scale red; above 30% the colour moves red -> orange -> amber and gets denser, so 40..100% no longer merge.
- Team health scale: the cap of 100 is now reached at 100000 t (was 30000 t), the curve is 100*(t/100000)^0.5 (was ^0.62 from 30000): 39 t = 2, 800 t = 9, 3190 t = 18, 9600 t = 31, 25860 t = 51, 32700 t = 57, 44891 t = 67, 100000 t = 100. Big ships no longer share one value, so Roma / Kongo / Nimitz differ in wound chance and strength. Config text TeamHealth updated (loc row 676, 8 languages).
- Wound table: Roma replaced by Yamato (ijn_bb_yamato, found by ini or by the name "yamato"); ships are sorted by displacement; a ship or ammunition missing from the game data / mods gets no column / row (a trace line says which). Added the destroyer Fletcher (usn_dd_fletcher, or the name "fletcher"). The ship name in the column header loses a trailing "(...)" part such as "(Fletcher-class)". Cells: the chance now has a "%" and the wound strength is followed by a drawn blood drop. The drop is 50% transparent. A white crosshair icon follows the chance. The crosshair is 50% transparent with a thinner line. Readability on strong cells: the amber end of the ramp is darker (white text reads on it), the small line goes from grey to light above 30%.
- Wound chance halved in the formula itself (power / 12 / sqrt(health), before the cut at 100%); the WoundChance option is unchanged (1.0).
- New option DamageControlTeams/ConcussionChance (GUI: Weapon hits, "Concussion chance", "x chance", 0..1000, default 1.0 as a usual multiplier; Base Game profile 1000 = practically always, as before; the key was HealTimeChance for a day, never released). A wounded team is concussed with the chance min(1, wound / health before the wound / 2 x ConcussionChance): the share of the team's current health the wound takes (a fresh team wounded by 20%: 10%; 40% left and a 20% wound: 25%). 0 = never; a game knock-out without the health model is always concussed. Trace: "DC team concussion: wound ..% of ..% health, chance ..% -> concussed / not concussed". Advanced.cfg HealTime is 180 s, Base Game 90 s. Loc: the description is the last row (677, 680, 681, 682, 683 obsolete), label 678, the unit reuses "x chance" (679 obsolete).
- (shell fire / one shell = one section) 1) ReserveHits.First(impact, key): a ConditionalWeakTable per ImpactData (blast x ship) remembers the sections already rolled; ProcessCompartmentHit is called per collider (flooding_<port|stbd>_<N>), so a section got 2-4 rolls per shell. Repeats are dropped before Skip("passed to the teams") in the AnyIntegrity branch and in the reserve branch (number == 3); WoundStats.Dup counts them ("same shell and section again N"). 2) ShellFire ([FireAndFlooding] ShellFireByPower bool; Advanced true, Base Game false): the game rolls a flat 20 % for the hit compartment (Blastzone.OnHitUnit, int percent, local set 30 then 20 for a projectile) and 20 x Rand(0.6, 0.9) for neighbours (ProcessCompartmentHit, float, only when the shell got through). AdmPlugin.PatchFireChance finds "ldc 30; stloc X" and the later "ldc 20; stloc X" in each method and wraps the 20 in ShellFire.OwnGame (returns 0 for a shell = the game's roll is off) / NeighbourGame (Percent(power, false)); expects exactly 1 hit per method (checked in the IL of 0.9.x: OnHitUnit IL_0a68, ProcessCompartmentHit IL_01e6; a Warn is written otherwise). The hit compartment's own roll is made in BlockedFire.Prefix (OwnFire), because only there arg[2] (blocked) is known: chance = 90 * min(1, warheadPower / 5), times BlockedHitFireChance (Advanced 0.5) if blocked (37 mm 17, 57 mm 27, 76 mm 43, 90 mm 54, 100 mm+ 90); growth = damage / FireIntegrity / 20, start severity GunFire.Own. Blocked neighbours: Percent(power) * Rand(0.6, 0.9) x BlockedHitFireChance. No separate multiplier option: the existing BlockedHitFireChance is the stopped-shell factor (user's decision). Incendiary shells (_addFire) and non-gun weapons stay the game's. texts: rows 691-692 (rows 685-690 obsolete), 8 languages. Not tested in a battle: check a trace for "same shell and section again" and the fire lines.
- (wounded in the middle section) A team wounded in the middle section (index 2 of 5, where the reserve is) walked "to the reserve" for the muster time (TransferTime(2) = Muster, min 10 s) although there is no distance. DcTeams.InjuredTime(comp, section): 0 s when the section is the middle one, else TransferTime; used by ApplySectionHit and the game's-knock-out branch. The team is back in the reserve in the same tick (concussion counted there). Seen in a trace as "DC team wounded (section 2 ...)".
- (hit list fire note, Auto DC cancel, concussion trace) 1) HitLog: the "started a fire" note was only set for a fire in a NEIGHBOUR of a stopped shell's compartment and shown only for stopped hits; the fire of the hit compartment itself (ShellFire, BlockedFire.OwnFire) and fires of shells that got through were not noted, so the list showed the fire at the wrong line. Now OwnFire calls HitLog.NoteBlockedFire, the Postfix keeps a Pending for every record, and the panel writes "(stopped by armour, started a fire)" / "(started a fire)". Fires the GAME starts in neighbours of a shell that got through are still not noted. 2) Auto DC never redirects a walking team (there is no such code; the earlier worry came from two ships' lines interleaved in one trace). A cancel of walking teams whose section needs nobody was tried and removed (the user: not a problem, not worth the risk). 3) Concussion lost on arrival in the reserve (reported for Kongo): not found by reading - Stun is only set in Concuss and only counted down in Pool. Suspect: Reconcile (a team object dropped / re-made when the game's counts differ). Reconcile now traces "a concussed team (N s left, state) is moved or dropped"; the arrival line "DC team back in the reserve ... concussion left N s" is there too. Needs a trace of a battle with wounds.
- (wounded vs concussed label) A wounded team walking back to the reserve (State Injured) was labelled "concussed, going to the reserve" in its tooltip and in the icon legend whatever the concussion roll gave; the concussion is only rolled (wound / health before / 2 x ConcussionChance), so most wounded teams were not concussed and looked as if they lost the status on arrival (seen in the user's Kongo trace: wound 2%, "not concussed", back in the reserve at once, sent to work 2 s later). Now the tooltip says "concussed, going to the reserve" only when Stun > 0, else "wounded, going to the reserve"; the legend says "wounded, going to the reserve (In Transfer)". Rows 694-695. Also: trace lines about teams carry the team number (#N).
- (a wounded team goes on working) DcTeams.ApplySectionHit: a wounded team used to walk to the reserve whatever the concussion roll gave (and was sent out again at once when fresh). Now: dead -> as before; concussed (Concuss returns true) -> leaves, walks to the reserve (Injured) and sits out its HealTime there; wounded and not concussed -> stays (in its section or on its way) with the lower health, trace "... wounded (section N), health X, goes on". Teams with health under RestHealth still go to rest through RestPull. The tooltip label "wounded, going to the reserve" of the previous item is now seen only for a team that is concussed... (Stun > 0), so it stays mostly unused. Base Game (ConcussionChance 1000): always concussed, as the game's knock-out. The game's own knock-out branch (AnyIntegrity off) is unchanged.

- (armour classes of the WW2 mod) New src/ArmorClasses.cs, [HullIntegrity] ArmorClasses (bool; Advanced true, Base Game false; Settings: Hull integrity group, mod only). Cause of "every shell penetrates the Kongo": ObjectBaseLoader.loadObjectFromInternalResources reads the hull's [General] ArmorType, lower-cased, and accepts only none/minor/moderate/heavy (BaseSystem.ArmorType 0..3), anything else = None; ObjectBase.GetPenetrationDamageMultiplier returns 1 for None. The WW2 mod's classes (LightCruiser, HeavyCruiser, BattleCruiser, BattleCruiser2, SuperHeavy; 103 of 238 hulls; the typo "Battlecruiser22" = BattleCruiser2) are only in its damage.ini [PenetrationVsArmorMatrix]. The original mod's damage_mod.dll (SeaPowerArmorSim 4.5.86, disabled because it conflicts with ADM) does not parse them either: it replaces the vanilla damage by plate geometry. Patches: a transpiler on the loader replaces the ToLower() after ldstr "ArmorType" by ArmorClasses.Read (remembers the class; hands the game moderate for LightCruiser and heavy for the rest, so without the postfix the hull is still armoured); a postfix sets obp._armorType to 4..8 (new enum values, not named in the game); a prefix on the private ObjectBase.getMinMaxDamageMultiplier answers for 4..8 from ADM's tables (min,max per penetration minor/moderate/heavy/always, numbers of the mod's damage.ini; the game's own types keep the game's matrix, i.e. the mod's if it is loaded). Other readers of the type are safe: Compartments.Init armour-bonus switch has a default branch (no integrity bonus for the new types), PointValueRoleScorers parses the string itself. Hull log line prints the class name. 3 new texts (rows 696-698), 8 languages.
- (armour classes: modules and the [TaskForce] fallback) Same switch. (1) The ArmorType of a module is read in two places, both transpiled to ArmorClasses.ReadModule (an empty value changes nothing, so a modules.ini record without the key does not wipe the class read from the ini; a stock name resets it): BaseSystem.LoadBaseParameters (ini: ldstr "ArmorType" ... ToLower) and ModuleRecord.TryGetArmorType (ToLowerInvariant on the record's string). The game gets the nearest stock class; a postfix on LoadBaseParameters sets BaseSystem._armorType to 4..8 (the penetration lookup for modules is the same GetPenetrationDamageMultiplier -> getMinMaxDamageMultiplier). Before, a module with SuperHeavy etc. (most turrets/magazines of the mod) was None. (2) usn_bb_iowa45.ini and usn_bb_missouri2.ini of the WW2 mod have the hull's ArmorType=Heavy in [TaskForce] (a typo), the game reads [General] only (default minor). The hull transpiler now replaces the IniHandler.readValue call by ArmorClasses.ReadHull: [General] first, then [TaskForce], then the game's default; trace "no ArmorType in [General], taken from [TaskForce]". The old text row 696 is obsolete, the new one is 699.
- (armour classes: tables follow the game's matrix) The user wants the base game's heavy/moderate/minor as the reference. The mod's own matrix has harder minor/moderate/heavy than the base game (its heavy mean 0.18 vs 0.43), so copying its numbers for the five classes put e.g. BattleCruiser2 below the base game's heavy. Now ArmorClasses.TableOf builds each class from the LIVE Globals._damageValues._penVsArmorMatrix (keys 1..3; base-game constants if unreadable): lerp(A, B, t) per cell, SuperHeavy = heavy x 0.686. Places taken from the mod's order by the mean multiplier (mod: SuperHeavy .12 < heavy .18 < BC2 .22 < BC .37 < moderate = HeavyCruiser .51 < LightCruiser .68 < minor .88) mapped onto the game's scale: LightCruiser = lerp(moderate, minor, 0.458), HeavyCruiser = moderate, BattleCruiser = lerp(heavy, moderate, 0.596), BattleCruiser2 = lerp(heavy, moderate, 0.131), SuperHeavy = 0.686 x heavy. With the base matrix the means are .30 / .43 (heavy) / .46 / .57 / .67 / .76 / .87; with the mod's matrix loaded the classes follow its heavy/moderate/minor. Trace line "armour classes: penetration tables from the game's matrix: ..." once per matrix. Text row 699 obsolete, new 700.
- (armour classes: retuned) The user found SuperHeavy too hard and BattleCruiser2 / HeavyCruiser too soft on the base game's scale. Recipes now (lerp from stock A to B by t, x Scale): LightCruiser moderate->minor 0.458 (mean 0.76); HeavyCruiser heavy->moderate 0.963 (0.66, no longer equal to moderate); BattleCruiser heavy->moderate 0.667 (0.59); BattleCruiser2 heavy->moderate 0.37 (0.52); SuperHeavy heavy x 0.902 (0.39). Base game: heavy 0.43, moderate 0.67, minor 0.87. Moderate (a stock class) is not touched: ADM does not change the game's own tables (0.67). Text row 700 obsolete, new 701.
- (armour classes: integrity bonus) Compartments.Init has a switch on obp._armorType: minor x1.2, moderate x1.25, heavy x1.3 on _maxIntegrity (the default branch, i.e. none and the new types: nothing). ArmorClasses.InitTranspiler (a second transpiler on Init, found by the 4-target switch after the _armorType load) inserts ArmorClasses.IntegrityBonus(this) at the switch's case-0 label, i.e. before ApplyHull (so the hull multiplier and OriginalX see the bonus, as for the game's classes). Even steps between the game's: SuperHeavy 1.3125, heavy 1.3, BattleCruiser2 1.2875, BattleCruiser 1.275, HeavyCruiser 1.2625, moderate 1.25, LightCruiser 1.225, minor 1.2. Text row 701 obsolete, new 702.
- (armour classes: values set by the user; '>' in his messages means "replace with", not "greater") Means (base game scale): SuperHeavy 0.39, BattleCruiser2 0.50, BattleCruiser 0.56, HeavyCruiser 0.62 (t from heavy to moderate: 0.286 / 0.540 / 0.793), LightCruiser 0.76 as before; moderate is not touched. Integrity bonus: SuperHeavy 1.3, BattleCruiser2 / BattleCruiser / HeavyCruiser 1.25, LightCruiser 1.2 (the even steps of the previous item are gone). Text row 702 obsolete, new 703.
- (armour classes: GUI hint) The bottom hint (the cfg description) of the row was too wordy for the settings window; cut to about 500 characters (row 706; rows 699-703 obsolete). Label and unit are as before.
- (Armour card on the DC tab) DamageControlUi.Panels.cs AddArmorCard: a card "Armour" in summary column 3 (HorizontalAlignment Right, against the hull card of column 4, whose column is made 190 px instead of 220; restored in RemoveExtras): title, the class name (the game's: None / Minor / Moderate / Heavy, translated; ours: LightCruiser ... SuperHeavy as is), under it "Hull integrity x1.3" when there is a bonus. The tooltip (made on MouseEnter, ArmorTip): per shell penetration (Minor / Moderate / Heavy / Always) the chance that the armour stops the shell (1 - max of the multiplier) and the damage multiplier range when it gets through. ArmorClasses.Info(comp, ...) gives the type, the bonus and the 8 numbers (the live matrix for the game's classes). CenterForecast treats the card as the right neighbour (the forecast is centred between the cards on the left and this one). Always shown (also with Alternative Damage Control off). Rows 707-712 (Always, Armour, Heavy, Hull integrity x{0}, Shell penetration:, the stops line), 8 languages.
- (armour card: class names) The names of the five new classes are shown translated (DamageControlUi.ArmorName: Light cruiser, Heavy cruiser, Battlecruiser, Battlecruiser II, Super heavy; rows 713-717), the Hull log line keeps the identifiers.
- (armour card tooltip: systems) Under the hull's table the tooltip lists the systems that have an armour of their own (BaseSystem._armorType > 0, from the module's ini / modules.ini): per class, strongest first (ArmorClasses.StrongestFirst), the display names with a count (up to 6 names, then "... +N"). Nothing when no system has armour. Row 718 "Systems:".
- (Critical state: fires) ContactIntel.HeavyLevel: the fire criterion is the MEAN fire severity over all sections (FireTotal / section count) >= 30%, was the total over the sections >= 50% (a Littorio with 9% hull damage and no water was "Critical" with three sections burning at 15 / 25 / 30%). The Fires card (Minor / Moderate / Severe) still goes by the total. The mod's own fire-fighting teams' rest rule that uses Heavy follows the same criterion.
- (armour tooltip) The damage when a shell gets through is shown in per cent as the mean +/- the spread ((min + max) / 2 +/- (max - min) / 2, whole per cent, not less than 1 unless exactly 0), not as the multiplier range. Row 719 (712 obsolete).
- (armour tooltip) The English source of the damage line has the real sign too: "damage {2} % ± {3} %" (row 720; 719 obsolete).
- (armour tooltip) No space before % in the damage line (row 721; 720 obsolete).
- (armour tooltip: a table) The hull's penetration lines are a Grid of 3 columns: shell penetration | Stops | Damage if penetrated (mean % ± spread %), header in grey. Rows 723-725 (Damage if penetrated (725; 722 obsolete), Shell penetration, Stops); the old line format (rows 712, 719-721) and "Shell penetration:" (711) are obsolete.
- (armour tooltip) "Systems:" groups names that differ only by a trailing _N (WeaponMagazine_203mm_1.._4 -> "4 x WeaponMagazine_203mm", count first), per armour class.
- (armour tooltip) The "Systems:" block: the armour class on its own line, then one system per line ("4 x Name", indent 14 px); up to 6 systems per class, the rest as "... +N".
- (armour classes: saved games) The multiplier saved next to the hull state (HullMult key) is now hull multiplier x integrity bonus of the new classes (HullSaveMult, ArmorClasses.BonusOf). Trace 05:17-05:21: a Kongo (BattleCruiser2, x1.25) loaded from a game saved without the bonus showed every section at 80 % (239.8 of 299.8 = x3 without the bonus) and the teams "repaired" it; a game saved before this change with the bonus on loads x1.25 too high once. Class changed or switched off between save and load: the integrities are rescaled by the ratio.
- (hit list) HitLog.Postfix traces "hit not in the list: <type> in <compartment>, damage N" (debug, 12 times) when ProcessCompartmentHit has no impact data of the hit object: the trace showed a power-32 hit (fires +14.8 / +14.3 / +15.8 % in 2 sections, team rolls "Hit on section 3/4: power 32") with no "hit" line and no entry in the list.
- (hit list: root cause found in the game's code) Blastzone.OnHitUnit sets ImpactData._hitCompartmentIndex / _portSideHit once, from the hit POINT (Utils.GetCompartmentIndex, sign of local x), while ProcessCompartmentHit runs for the COLLIDERS the blast touched (flooding_<side>_<n>), which need not be that compartment (a hit near a section boundary or the centre line, a shell through the deck). HitLog.Postfix made a record only when collider == hit point's section and side, so such a shell (the 381 mm of the trace: fires in 3 compartments, team rolls in sections 3 and 4, power 32) never reached the list. Now one record per shell (impact data), made at the first compartment processed and moved to the hit compartment when that one comes (HitLog._made). The trace line says "(a neighbour of the hit point's N side)". Each shell has its own Blastzone and map: two shells in one frame do not interfere.
- (hit list: a hit on a mast) Trace 05:50-05:51: a hit on Kongo's mast ("hit postfixes: calls 3", a team sent to section 2 for systems) made no record. The game's ProcessCompartmentHit starts by splitting the collider's name and returns at once unless it is "flooding_<side>_<n>" (IL: Split('_'), length < 3 / not "flooding" / TryParse fails -> ret), so a mast, turret or system collider gives no compartment processing and HitLog.Postfix saw nothing. New postfix on Blastzone.OnHitUnit (GunHitPost -> HitLog.HitUnitPost): when the shell has impact data but no record yet, the record is made on the hit point's section and side (_hitCompartmentIndex / _portSideHit; trace "(no compartment collider touched: a mast, a turret or a system)"); a flooding collider processed later still moves it to the hit compartment (HitLog._made).
- (hit list: the place of a system hit) Trace 06:40:52 + screenshot: a 381 mm SAP hit listed as "Midships, starboard" (section of the hit POINT) while the damaged systems (10m GFCS, Type 94 Mod.1 GFCS) stand in Forward Midships - a mast's hit point and its systems can be in different sections. OnHitUnit calls BaseSystem.AddDamage(float) for the system it hit (IL_1999); new prefix on it (HitLog.SystemDamaged) notes the first system damaged inside the OnHitUnit in progress (HitLog.HitUnitPre/_curSys, set from the OnHitUnit prefix GunHitPre), and HitUnitPost puts the record on that system's section and side (SectionOfSystem: the system compartment list, the mount / collider position as in ExplodePost); trace "(system hit: <name>)". A flooding collider processed later still moves the record to the hit compartment.
- (hit list: a system hit shows the system) When the shell touched no hull compartment, the list names the damaged system ("10m GFCS") instead of "<section>, <side>" (HitLog.Rec.System; saved as the 5th field of a record, an index in the names). A hull compartment processed later clears the name and puts the section and side back.

(2.0.0) Release 2.0.0 prepared. Game 0.9.2 (gamefiles 4190ba7): the diff 7a9eaba..4190ba7 touches no type ADM patches (checked by decompiling both Seapower-Scripts.dll; all 98 type-name literals resolve in 0.9.2; no `BaseObject` literal in src; `SeapowerUI.ViewModels.X` literals are full type names, `"SeaPower"` in ShipNames.cs is the namespace only). Loader: R.A.T.S. Keel (recommended, Workshop 3835527459) or Anchor Chain (3380210757); `Release/_info.ini` got `[Keel] StartEarly=true` and the requirement text (en, ru); README.md / README.txt / README.BBCode.txt (7782 bytes): bold "Loader changed in 2.0.0" in Compatibility and both links in Install step 1. `src/SettingsHub.cs` is byte-identical to the ASE copy (7d3319a). Version 2.0.0 in csproj, PluginVersion, PUBLISHING, READMEs; ChangeList.txt / ChangeList-1000.txt (580 chars) list the net change since the published 1.9.0 (0.9.0-only, profile line, debug trace, small ships, explosions, duds, HullFireVulnerability and the 75/50% rule were already in 1.9.0). Lang: the 6 keys missing from the 8 translations are all rows marked "obsolete" in en.tsv (607, 610, 611, 673, 677, 705), so every live text is translated. Not tried in the game.

(2.0.0) The mod no longer polls its .cfg or the profile file on disk (removed FileWatch, WatchConfigFile, ReloadConfig, ReloadProfile and the stamps in SaveConfig / profile writes). Settings change through the settings window or Configuration Manager (SettingChanged); a hand edit of a file is read at the next start of the game.

(2.0.0) Speed-ups from a static review (not measured in the game; ProfilePerformance slots Heavy / FireHooks / SecTick / DeckCorners show the effect): (1) FlameUpMulCore (FireGrowthLimit) and CheckFirePostfixCore read/write the fire fields through Fld<float> / Prop<float> / Fld<Array> (Fast.cs: _gScGrow, _gScMaxSev, _gScMaxGrow, _gScSeverity, _gCompSys) instead of FieldInfo.GetValue / SetValue; (2) ContactIntel.HeavyLevel is worked out once per frame and ship (ConditionalWeakTable memo, Time.frameCount), AnyLeak makes no array per call; (3) SectionEffects.Tick indexes the section arrays as object[] (Array.GetValue only as a fallback); (4) DeckCorners.Tick sets the line width and colour only when they changed. Left as they are: Collisions.Spill / Left (only during a collision), ApplyHideModel (debug view, once a second), SubmergedFlooding array rebuild (only when the arrays change).

(2.0.0) Leak review (static). Fixed: DcTeams.HeavyUntil was a Dictionary keyed by the ship's Compartments (strong: every ship of every mission stayed in memory) - now a ConditionalWeakTable; DamageControlUi._compacted (HashSet of the game's UI elements) - weak table; DeckCorners.RebuildVisual made a new HideAndDontSave Material per selected ship, a new label Mesh (old one not destroyed) and `lr.material` copies per line - now one shared material, the old label mesh destroyed, `sharedMaterial`; the collider wireframe meshes (ours) are destroyed with their overlay (the fill's mesh is the game's); ArmorClasses.Kept (unused strong dictionary) removed. Checked and fine: all other per-ship state is in ConditionalWeakTable, the WeakReference lists are pruned (LiveHulls, Views, Decorated, StatusViews, DcTabs, pause buttons), the string-keyed caches are bounded (Told 200, _bwCache 500, _skips / _warnedOnce / _ghostSeen by distinct names), the ghost materials / opaque textures are destroyed on restore, WeaponStatusColors subscriptions are disposed in Detach, UiScan lists drop on Unloaded.

(2.1.0) Release 2.1.0: version bumped (csproj, PluginVersion, READMEs, PUBLISHING); loaders listed Anchor Chain first, R.A.T.S. Keel second and marked "(temporarily unavailable)" in Install; no "(recommended)" any more. `[Keel] StartEarly=true` in _info.ini stays. Changes since 2.0.0: the speed-ups, the leak fixes, no polling of .cfg / profile files (see the notes above).

(2.1.0) Advanced.cfg defaults changed to the author's: Warships 2.5, CivilianShips 1.5, CounterFloodLimit 70, TransferSpeed 75.

(2.1.1) Minor release: only the new Advanced.cfg defaults (see above); version bumped everywhere.

Collisions: a contact with a unit that has the LifeRaft role (Role=Deco,LifeRaft in all_life_raft*.ini; a vessel for the game) is skipped in StepCore (Collisions.IsRaft, memo per Compartments): no slowing, no hull damage, no push from the mod; the game's own handling stays. all_assault_raft has Role=Deco only (not a LifeRaft) and is still handled as a ship.

(2.1.2) Minor release: collisions skip life rafts; version bumped everywhere.

## Settings window: one-line tab strip and screenshots of the tabs (settings kit, after the last release, not committed)
The tabs of the settings window are one line: `TabScroll` (src/TabScroll.cs, shared by all rgreat mods) puts the panel of tabs into a ScrollViewer without bars, arrow buttons ◀ ▶ at both ends are visible only while the tabs do not fit, the wheel over the strip scrolls it, and the chosen tab is kept in view (`Reveal`: when it is chosen, when the window gets smaller, when the arrows appear or go). Debug tab, "Screenshot all tabs now" (a button, `Kind.Action`): `SettingsShots.Run` (src/SettingsShotKit.cs, shared) goes through every tab (and every shown inner tab of DC Teams), fits the window to the tab (largest, then the width of the widest row, then the height of the options) and writes the PNG files to BepInEx/AlternativeDamageModel_screens; `ShotAdapter` in SettingsWindow.cs is this mod's `IShotWindow`. TabScroll.cs and SettingsShotKit.cs are copies of the files in the SeaPower.AlternativeBase repository (sibling folder; tools/sync.sh copies them to the mods): change them there, not here.

## Settings window frame and row editors (settings kit: SettingsKit.cs, after the last release)
- The window chrome is no longer in `SettingsWindow.cs`: `src/SettingsKit.cs` (THE SAME FILE in every rgreat mod; canonical copy in the SeaPower.AlternativeBase repository, copied by its `tools/sync.sh`, never edited in a mod) has `WinStyle` (colours `Bg/Panel/Card/Line/Text/Dim/Warn/Title/Field/Hl`, `B`, `Cross`, `TryNum`, `GroupTitle`) and `WinFrame` (one instance `_f` per window: `Root`, `Move`, `Host`, `Popup`, `Top`, `W`, `H`).
- `WinFrame`: `Locate(anchor, other)` finds the panel (topmost Grid / Canvas that is not an items host) and sets `Top`; `Mount(panel)` puts `Root` into it (or into a Popup), `Unmount()` takes it out, `Attached` says whether it is on a screen; `NewRoot / Window / TitleBar / Card / HelpPane / Footer / Assemble` build the window; `OnLayout` converts the size from screen pixels to UI units once (width AND height, the first time), cuts it to the screen less 40 px and centres it; `Draggable`, edges (5 px) and corners (16 px) for resizing (`MinW/MinH/MaxW/MaxH`, AGI sets `MinW = 520`); `Typing(on)` switches the game's hot keys (InputHandler.typingActive), AGI replaces it with `Keyboard` (the plugin's `SetTyping`); `Tr` is applied to the texts given to `Text/Btn` (ADM: `Loc.T`, its English text is the key).
- Editors: `Number` (− / box / + with unit, `commits` hook for AGI's commit-on-Apply), `Check`, `Combo` (index + pick, refresh does not call pick), `ResetButton`, `Hover` (help pane text of the row under the mouse). The mods keep the fields, where a value is read and written (`Get / Set`), the rows that are special (lists, tables, pads, ships, profiles).
- In each mod's `SettingsWindow.cs` the old copies of `Text / Btn / Edit / Cross / Draggable / OnLayout / ResizeHandle / Typing` are gone; one-line forwarders `Text / Btn / Edit / Typing / TryNum / Num / GroupTitle / Cross / SafeParent` remain so the rest of the code did not change. `_root / _move / _host / _top / _winW / _winH` became `_f.Root / _f.Move / _f.Host / _f.Top / _f.W / _f.H`.
- Small changes of behaviour: ASE and AWC now take the width from screen pixels too (it was 820 UI units, the same at scale 1); ADM's resize handler (events stop while resizing) is now in every mod; a drop-down does not call its handler when the window refreshes it.
- `SettingsHub.cs` is canonical in the base repository now (it was in ASE); the four copies are identical.

## Switch between the mods' settings windows (after the last release)
- Each mod keeps its own settings window; at the top of every window a row shows the names of the installed, ticked rgreat mods (`WinFrame.ModRow`, only with two or more). A click hides this window KEEPING its edits (`SettingsWindow.Hide` -> `_kept = true`) and opens the other mod's window at the same place and size (the rectangle goes through AppDomain data `rgreat.settings.v1.geom`, taken by `WinFrame.Mount`). Open skips the reload while `_kept`, so the edits come back.
- Hub registry entries got fields [6] dirty, [7] commit, [8] discard, [9] hide, [10] kept (`SettingsHub.Register` overload; readers of the old six fields still work). `Commit` calls `SettingsHub.CommitOthers(SelfId)` first (kept windows of the other mods are applied: `CommitKept` = `Commit(true)`), `Cancel` / ✕ call `DiscardOthers`. A yellow dot on a mod's name = edits not applied there (`UpdateModDots`, called where the window's own changed markers are updated).
- Not done: the context the window was opened with (AGI: from which game window) is not carried over a switch; AGI opens with every tab, as from the menu.
- The list of mods in a small window (the hub's `ShowList`) is gone: the menu button of several mods opens the window of the mod used last (AppDomain data `rgreat.settings.v1.last`, else the first in order); the row of the mods at the top of the window switches.

## Tabs, search, revert (kit)

The settings window has a search box and a revert button (the edits of the tab not applied yet) at the right of the tab strip, and a ↺ on every group title with edits (to the saved values, not the defaults). Search shows the options of all the tabs that are shown. Pages built again in place keep the scroll position (WinFrame.KeepScroll). See SeaPower.AlternativeBase README, "Tabs, search, revert".
ADM tabs: "Effects" is now "Sinking". The strip has User Interface, Sinking, Damage Model, DC Teams, Debug; "Damage Model" has a second row of pages (General, Hull, Systems, Fire, Flooding, Collisions). `Tabs` stays the flat list of pages (Field.Tab, the screenshots); `Top` / `TopNames` group them for the strip.

## Ctrl+C copies the text under the mouse (kit)
While the settings window is on a screen, Ctrl+C copies the help text (name - text, and the yellow extra line) of the row under the mouse, or what is written in the help pane while the mouse is over it (`GUIUtility.systemCopyBuffer`); a small "✓" shows in the pane after the copy. It does nothing while the text box that has the keyboard has text selected (its own Ctrl+C copies that; note a box selects all of its text when it gets focus). Done by `CopyPoll`, a MonoBehaviour created in `Mount` and destroyed in `Unmount` (polls `Input.GetKeyDown(C)`), no per-mod hook. The kit now needs the references `UnityEngine.InputLegacyModule` and `UnityEngine.IMGUIModule` in the csproj of every mod. Not verified in game.

## One shared Language (hub + kit)
The Language setting is no longer a row of each mod: it is a drop-down at the right of the mods' row (kit `ModRow`; the row is shown also with one mod), "Default" = the game's own language. The hub holds the value (`SettingsHub.SharedLanguage` / `SetLanguage` / `JoinLanguage`, AppDomain data `rgreat.settings.v1.lang` and `.langsinks`): every mod joins at start with a sink that stores the code in its own `[General] Language` and refreshes `Loc`; the first mod to start brings its stored value, the others take the shared one. A change is applied at once (not with Apply) and the window rebuilds itself (`WinFrame.LanguageChanged`, supplied by each mod: AGI `RebuildAfterLayout`, AWC / ASE `_redraw`, ADM `RebuildAfterLanguage`, which puts the window back in the middle). The mods give the list (`WinFrame.Languages`) and the caption (`LanguageLabel`). The window is at least as wide as the row needs for up to 4 mod names plus the language (`_rowW`; wider windows wrap when there are more mods). Not verified in game.
